| D187 | 2026-11-11 | The wake-phrase harness and its smoke run; the acceptance is a PC step (WO-5.4, PM08) | `gos voice wake-eval` (`gregtec_voice.wake_eval`) runs the real engine (`WakeWord`: stage 1, the VAD, stage 2 inline, the cancel window) block by block on the audio's own clock, so its counts don't depend on the CPU. A **hit** (`asked`) is an attempt whose question was captured: it chimed, then ended once in end of speech with no cancel and (synthetic attempts, where the question's start is known) after the question started; attempts that only chimed are reported separately (`chimed`), and each miss says why (`not detected`, `rejected`, `no question`, `no end of speech`, `woke twice`, `question cut off`). A **false wake** is a chime in the negatives. Detections and false wakes are also counted at stage 1. **Synthetic** (`--kokoro DIR`): each candidate of `tests/evals/wake_phrases.tsv` (`phrase note near`; 8 candidates: [H§1.3]'s "hey archivist" and "okay tessera", "hey tessera", "hey foreman", "okay vesper", "hello quartermaster", and two controls, "hey jarvis" and "hey computer") is said 40 times by Kokoro v1.0 (kokoro-onnx, not a dependency: a development and PC tool; Apache-2.0 weights) in 8 voices at speeds 0.9-1.3, each followed by a question of the STT set and mixed over generated noise (quiet, game-like rumble with tones and sweeps at 15 and 6 dB SNR, keyboard clicks, PC hum, other voices); the negatives are the 70 open-mic lines of `tests/evals/wake_chatter.txt` (GregTech/Gregtec talk, "hey"/"okay" phrases, near misses of every candidate) and the 24 near misses, said by the same voices over noise, then `--noise-minutes` of noise alone. Nothing but the report is written (no audio, no model, `data/reports/wake-eval-<time>.tsv`). **Recorded** (`--audio DIR`, Clayton's PC): `DIR/wake/*.wav` are attempts (`gos voice test --save`; 2 s of silence is added after each, as a talk-key recording ends too soon after the question for end of speech), `DIR/play/*.wav` open-mic play (`gos voice capture`, new, Windows: the microphone to a 16 kHz WAV); the configured phrase is scored against plan 8's targets (exit 0 only when >= 38/40 asked and <= 1 false wake/h). **Smoke run** (this container, re-run on the reviewed code: no GPU, microphone or voice; sherpa-onnx at threshold 0.15, stage 2 Whisper base.en int8 on the CPU without the prompt, with D186's word check; 15.1 minutes of negatives, 10 of them noise alone; 22 minutes for all 8; stage-1 / chimed / asked of 40): "hey archivist" 39 / 39 / 34, median detection 111 ms after the phrase, 1 stage-1 false wake ("hey, archive this", rejected by stage 2), 0 false wakes; its 5 chimed-not-asked are all babble clips (`no end of speech`: Silero hears the 12 dB background voices as speech, so the question never ends; on Clayton's headset, friends' voices don't reach the microphone, so the PC run is the real measure); "hello quartermaster" 39 / 37 / 32, 199 ms, 0 and 0 (the word check rejected 2 noisy attempts that the plain match had passed; 5 babble `no end of speech`); "hey jarvis" 38 / 36 / 33, 1 false wake ("Hey, Jarvis Cocker is a singer"); "hey foreman" 38 / 33 / 31, 0 false; "okay vesper" 37 / 23 / 21 (stage 2 rejected 14 of its 37 detections), 0 false; "hey computer" 40 / 34 / 28, 1 false wake ("hey, compute it"); no attempt of any phrase was `question cut off`; **"okay tessera" 0/40 and "hey tessera" 1/40**: sherpa's KWS spots the phrase as spelt in English word pieces, and a made-up word whose spelling doesn't say how it sounds isn't heard (the word pieces of "TESSERA" didn't match the voices' sound of it), so PM08's candidates must be ordinary English spellings. So the provisional default is "hey archivist" (D186), with "hello quartermaster" next; both controls woke on a chatter line, as [H§1.3] warns for common words. 15 minutes can't show <= 1 false wake an hour (one false wake is 4/h) and synthetic voices aren't Clayton's: it is a screening, not the acceptance. **The acceptance** is `docs/runbooks/wake.md` on the PC: 40 attempts in Clayton's voice, 2 hours of open-mic Discord play captured and scored with large-v3-turbo on his GPU, then live with `gos voice run` and `gos voice bench`'s wake counts; the phrase is his choice there (**pending Clayton**). The wake path's PM14 latency needs text-to-speech (WO-5.3) and is WO-5.7's benchmark; the engine adds the 0.6 s end-of-speech wait PM14 allows for. Tests: unit tests with fakes (the state machine, the match, the transcript, models and downloads, the harness and the CLI); `tests/integration/test_wake_models.py` (`slow`, skipped unless the models are present) runs the real KWS, Silero VAD, Whisper stage 2 and openWakeWord on ONNX | Records WO-5.4's evidence; adds `gos voice wake-eval` and `gos voice capture`; the acceptance stays open until the PC run | WO-5.4 |
| D216 | 2026-11-11 | M1 file mailbox, the OC side (WO-7.3) | `oc/mailbox.lua` is the agent's M1 transport, the interface of the HTTP one (`name`, `implicit_ack` false, `exchange(body, seq)`, `stats`, `close`), loaded when `transport` is `mailbox`, or `auto` with no internet card. **Folder:** `mailbox.dir` (default `mailbox/` next to the config, e.g. `/home/gos/mailbox`), absolute, letters, digits, `_ . -`, on a **managed**, writable drive, never `/tmp` (a RAM disk) nor a drive's top; refused with the reason otherwise; at start it logs the `drive` and `dir` gos.toml needs. **Send:** while `outbox.json` exists, poll `exists` every 0.25 s up to `wait_s` (default 2, 0-4; then the exchange fails as "the core hasn't taken the last message", the seq is spent and the core counts a gap); open `outbox.tmp` `"w"`, write, close; rename to `outbox.json`, 8 tries 50 ms apart while it returns false. **Receive:** `exists` (direct), `size` over `reply_max` deletes it unread, else read in 2048-byte chunks, close, remove (the acknowledgement); a remove that fails leaves it to be read again (harmless). An exchange first takes a reply left from the last one, then sends, then waits up to `wait_s` for the reply; no reply in time returns an empty ok reply with no ack, and the reply comes with the next exchange. Costs: one rename and at most one remove (a tick each); `list` and `lastModified` are never used. **Judgement calls (PR questions):** (1) the core acknowledges by renaming `outbox.json` **back to `outbox.tmp`**, not by deleting it: OC's drive adds the bytes of every new file OC writes to its used-space count and never sees a file deleted from outside until the world reloads [F§2.3], so a new file every 5 s fills the count within about an hour, while writing over the old `outbox.tmp` keeps it flat (`tests/oc/test_mailbox.lua` shows both). OC's `remove` of `inbox.json` subtracts fileCost and the size of a file OC never counted, so the count **falls** by about 0.5-2.5 KB an exchange and can go below zero within hours; that is harmless (deleting to acknowledge would instead grow it by each message's size less the reply's), but it comes from `fakeoc.lua`'s model of OC, so the Lab run (runbook section H, `df` before and after) checks it against the real OC; (2) a reply's `ack` always comes with the `boot` it counts in (schema: `boot` added to `#/$defs/reply`, `dependentRequired: {ack: [boot]}`), and an ack for another boot acknowledges nothing, so a reply left in `inbox.json` from an earlier run can't drop this run's waiting results. Licence: original code (MIT, this repo); OC 1.12.68 (MIT) and OpenOS were read for behaviour only. |
| D217 | 2026-11-11 | M1, the core side, and the shared OC ingest (WO-7.3 for WO-7.1) | **Ingest** (`core/gregtec/bridge/ingest.py`), the interface WO-7.1's `POST /oc/telemetry` adopts: `Bridge.receive(raw, transport, source, binding, written_at, now) -> Outcome` (256 KB cap, one JSON object, `v` 1, schema check; refusals `bad_payload`, `unsupported_version`, `internal`, never echoing payload text), `Bridge.reply(outcome, ack, poll_s) -> (doc, command ids)` (at most 2048 bytes; commands that don't fit or that the schema refuses go back), `Bridge.give_back(ids, why)`; protocols `Sink.ingest(Received)` (default `Latest`, each agent's newest message in memory, until WO-7.1/7.4) and `Commands.take/returned` (WO-7.1's queue; `NoCommands` until then; a returned command is sent again **under its id**). **Agent:** `AgentId(binding, address)`: `binding` is who the transport vouches the sender is, never taken from the message (a mailbox: its gos.toml role, `lab` or `play`; WO-7.1's HTTP endpoint: the instance its token is issued for), `address` the message's `computer.address`. One core watches Lab and Play, Lab runs any Lua gos-dev puts there (D75), and a Lab world copied from Play has Play's addresses, so a Lab message naming Play's computer is another agent: it never dedupes against Play's messages, replaces Play's latest message or takes Play's commands (`Commands.take(AgentId, limit)`: a command is confirmed for one binding's computer). **Dedupe:** `Seqs` keeps per agent (64 agents at most) its newest `boot` and highest `seq`; a message at or below it is answered and acked, not recorded again; a new boot starts again; skipped seqs are counted as `seq_gaps`. One `Bridge` per core (`Core.oc`), shared by every transport. **Mailbox** (`core/gregtec/bridge/mailbox.py`): gos.toml `[mailbox.lab]` / `[mailbox.play]` (`world`, default `[paths] lab_world` / `world`; `drive`, the address the agent prints; `dir`, default `home/gos/mailbox`); as gos-dev `[mailbox.play]` is refused (D75). A bad table is a service problem like any service key: `gos serve` prints `Not starting GOS: <reason>` and the core and supervisor refuse to start (exit 2) until it is fixed. One daemon thread polls every 0.25 s: `lstat` `outbox.json` (a link or non-file is left alone; over 256 KB is refused unread), `safe_open.read_bytes` (open, read, close, share-delete; a read that isn't one whole JSON object is retried 3 times, then refused `bad_payload`), ingest, then `os.replace` it back to `outbox.tmp` (D216); a rename that fails leaves it for the next poll, where it reads as a repeat. The reply: only while there is no `inbox.json`; stale `inbox.tmp` removed, `inbox.tmp` opened `"xb"` (never through a link), write, flush, fsync, close, `os.replace` to `inbox.json`; each step retried on `PermissionError` 6 times from 20 ms doubling, then the next poll with nothing changed and the reply's commands given back. Before each write the folder is resolved again and must be exactly `<instance>/<minecraft or .minecraft>/saves/<world>/opencomputers/<drive>/<dir>` with no link or junction anywhere below the instance; it writes nothing else. That check precedes the step, so a folder swapped for a junction in between (gos-dev racing it) could redirect that one step; after each step the core checks every folder from the instance down is still the same one (device and file id) and, if not, stops that mailbox until a restart with an error in the log. **Residual (accepted):** one step (a rename of an `outbox.json`, a reply written, a withdrawal) can land in another folder Clayton can write before that stop; POSIX `dir_fd` calls would close it but only on Linux, where nothing runs it, and Windows has no `dir_fd`. A stats line every 5 minutes and at stop. **Judgement calls (PR questions):** (1) **30 s reply TTL:** a reply OC hasn't taken in 30 s (3 cycles at OC's slowest; the game paused or the agent stopped) is removed and its commands go back; if OC holds it (Windows refuses the remove) it is being read and stays; (2) the ack is the highest seq of the agent's newest boot, not the seq of the message answered, so a repeat can never move it back; (3) **2 s quiet after a withdrawal:** OC removes `inbox.json` a tick after closing it, so a reply withdrawn while OC was reading it (Linux: the remove succeeds on an open file; Windows: right after OC's close) and followed at once by a new reply would have the new one removed unread; no reply is written for `QUIET_S` (2 s, far longer than a tick) after a withdrawal, which closes that on both OSes. |

the golden `tests/fixtures/oc/reply-ack.json`). **Python:** `tests/unit/test_oc_ingest.py` (parse, refusals, reply sizing, and a dedupe property over 12 seeds: zero, one or several deliveries per message, skipped seqs, restarts: each message recorded at most once, every first delivery recorded, the ack always the newest boot's highest seq); `tests/unit/test_oc_mailbox.py` (temp-dir round trips, half-written and oversize files, TTL withdrawal, `PermissionError` retries, links and junctions refused, config; and **Windows-only CI tests with real held handles**: `os.replace` onto a held `inbox.json`, a held reply not withdrawn, a held `outbox.tmp`, `safe_open` reading a held `outbox.json`); | D219 | 2026-11-11 | OC telemetry in the core and `POST /oc/telemetry` (WO-7.1) | **Endpoint** (`core/gregtec/bridge/http.py`, one `add_routes` call in the core's app): always HTTP 200 with one reply of at most 2048 bytes, built by WO-7.3's shared ingest (`Bridge.receive`/`reply`, D217); a refusal is `{v: 1, ok: false, cmds: [], err}` and processes nothing. Order: OC token (D221) -> per-token rate -> `Content-Type: application/json` -> `Content-Length` and the streamed body (at most 256 KB, refused unread past it) -> the schema. `unauthorized` for no or a wrong token; `bad_payload` for a wrong content type, an oversize, non-JSON or schema-invalid body; `unsupported_version`; `internal` for **more than a burst of 10 then one message a second per token** (judgement call: the contract's four errors are kept, and `internal` makes the agent back off, where a new `rate_limited` would change the schema and the agent) or a failure to record. An `ok: true` reply carries `boot` and `ack` (the newest boot's highest seq) as M1's do. **Tables** (`core/gregtec/bridge/telemetry.py`, the ingest's sink for both transports; DDL now also in the core's `store.py`): `tel_machine` one row per machine and power entry, `id` `<binding>/<component address>`, GT's meta name, `x y z`, `last_seen` (when the core received it), `status` `running`/`paused`/`idle`/`power`/`stale`; `mte_id` and `dim` stay NULL (no OC method gives them, D214; the meta-name map is WO-7.4's). `tel_sample(ts, src, key, value, text)`: computer readings (`mem_*`, `energy*`, `tick_ms`), machine readings (`active allowed work progress progress_max eu eu_max`, booleans as 1/0), power (`eu eu_max eu_in eu_out`, the LSC's exact `eu_str`/`eu_max_str` as text), AE2 (`cpus`, `cpus_busy`, `item:<registry name>@<damage>` sizes). **Judgement call: a sample is written when its value changes, and again every 60 s while it doesn't**, so a gap longer than that is a gap in the data (writing every reading every 5 s would be about 4 million rows a day for 32 machines); kept 7 days, pruned hourly. A reading the agent left out is not written; nothing is carried forward: **a machine's readings are only those its newest message carried** (review M1; kept in memory per machine; after a restart, until its next message, the keys with a sample within 60 s of its `last_seen`, which the heartbeat guarantees for a key still carried), so a failed call never shows an old value under the machine's fresh age. **At most 16 OC computers per binding** until the core restarts (review L5): a 17th address is refused with `internal`, so a token can't fill the core with made-up computers; an unauthenticated POST still costs one small indexed read of the live OC tokens (throttling before that check would let a flood of bad requests lock out the real computers). **Citations:** `telemetry_get` rows cite `tel:<binding>/<address>`; `tel:` is a citation in the fast lane's number check and in both lanes' prompts (review M2), beside r:/q:/mte:/crop:/plan:/w:. **Staleness:** a machine or agent not heard for 30 s (three cycles at OC's slowest) is `stale` (swept every second, and shown with `age_s` by `telemetry_get` and the dashboard's `oc` state: `connected`, `disconnected` once every agent is stale, `not_set_up` before any spoke). Sensor-line parsing (Problems counts) stays WO-7.4/7.5's. | Builds on D214, D217 | WO-7.1 |

Say confirm.", the name from the game DB by registry name and damage with no NBT). One pending action: a new proposal cancels the old and its read-back starts "Cancelled: <old>.". **Confirm** only with `via` in `voice dashboard phone discord`, which only the core passes: the router's instant "confirm" (the voice client's token is `voice`; a paired browser on `[core] dashboard_host` is `dashboard`, on any other allowed host `phone`; the Discord bot's token `discord`; admin `gos ask` and other clients are told to confirm another way) and `POST /api/actions/{id}/confirm` (the approval card's button, WO-9.1/9.3's buttons), which confirms only if that id is the pending one. **A plain "confirm" (the router's instant command, the voice hotkey) won't take a proposal younger than 3 s** (review M3): it names no action, and an async deep-lane turn may have just proposed or replaced one Clayton hasn't heard read back; it answers "Just proposed: <read-back>. Say confirm." or "A new action just replaced it: <read-back>. Say confirm." and the action stays pending. A button confirms by id at once. `dashboard` versus `phone` is read from the request's Host header, so any paired browser can pick either label: it is a log label only, never authority (review L4). When WO-9.1's Discord bot relays channel text to `/api/ask`, it must forward only Clayton's messages, or someone else's "confirm" would confirm as `discord`. Admin may cancel, not confirm. No tool can confirm (the tool schema has no such field; a static test checks `.confirm(` is called only from the router), and an OC message only finishes actions sent to its own binding and computer. **Expiry:** 60 s after proposal (an expired one says "That expired..." and can't be confirmed). **Delivery:** a confirmed `waypoint` is written at once by the core (`bridge/waypoint.py`: JourneyMap 5.2.23's `journeymap/data/sp/<world>/waypoints/<name>_<x>,<y>,<z>.json`, Nether x/z at x8, through no link, a new file only; as gos-dev never Play's; any error from the writer fails the action rather than leaving it confirmed (review L2), and the world folder's chain is checked for links before the `waypoints` folder is created (review L3); V23 stays open);

| D189 | 2026-11-11 | The recipe graph builder's rules (WO-3.3) | **Pending reviewer (PR question).** `gregtec.planner.graph` builds §7.1 step 2 from the newest export run. **Candidates:** enabled, not fake, except GT's assembly-line recipes, which GT lists only in its fake NEI map `gt.recipe.fakeAssemblylineProcess`: admitted, flagged `research`, when a scanner or research-station recipe showing the output is at the tier cap (the lowest such recipe is cited). NEI-only recipes in a GT category are rejected (D142 (1)); crafting-table and furnace recipes are kept, flagged `manual`. The machine check is WO-3.2's `machines.choose` with one and two plain energy hatches of the tier cap (D175: what runs at a tier runs above it); badged machines count, and a recipe only badged machines run carries the badge as a graph warning (D142 (a)). Recipes with EU/t 0 (the neutron activator, the Eye of Harmony) are kept when a machine runs them, flagged `eu0`. **Heuristic** (`graph.heuristic`, §7.1 with keys the harness DB showed are needed): recycling, GT's container maps (canner, packager, unpackager) and `eu0` last (D142 (2)); then the reverse of one of the item's uses last: a recipe that consumes something a recipe that uses the item up makes, when that has no way of its own (no main-product recipe, container maps and recycling aside, that doesn't use the item up): sulfuric acid from zinc sulfate, sodium from the sodium oxide only naquadah's line gives off; not salt water from salt and water (salt has its own routes), and not water or hydrogen given off by a use; raw inputs and boundaries don't count (review M1 and the final check: a main-product-only key flagged the salt water mixer and pulled naquadah's line into the epoxid graph); avoid chanced-only; lower tier; the item as the recipe's main product before a byproduct route (review M2); recipes that consume fluid cells after those that don't (a tie-break after tier); fewer consumed inputs; base machine (D12); machine before manual; unbadged; key. The machine check runs lazily in that order until 4 are valid; the rest are listed `unchecked` (D61's "show all" checks one when picked). **Expansion:** every item node keeps its full candidate list with a status (chosen, alternative, pruned, unchecked, rejected + reason), but only primary nodes expand more than their first: the target and its direct inputs expand their top 4 except *side routes* (ranked last, a reverse use, or the item only as a byproduct), which stay listed as alternatives a pin swaps in, unless every top recipe is one (then the first); a chain template's items expand the template's recipes, and its branch points are primary, only when the template chooses the target's recipe or a direct input's (the template is on the route); met anywhere else, a template's first pick is used and it adds no primary choice, except on one of the template's loops (an item its template recipes make back from itself, the platline's calcium) entered from outside the chain: that is left to the heuristic, since calcium for an epoxid's calcium acetate is no reason to run the platline (final check). Every other node expands its first, and swapping it is a pin (D61), which rebuilds. Expanding the top 4 everywhere made the platline graph thousands of nodes and seconds to build, and expanding side routes pulled the platline into nitric and sulfuric acid graphs through its nitrogen dioxide byproduct and a zinc sulfate reversal. **Boundaries:** request boundary, My base stock and line outputs (D63), `data/curated/raw_inputs.tsv` (water, lava, air: taken from the world although GT has byproduct recipes for them), items no recipe makes (`raw`), items whose every recipe was rejected for this request (`locked`: above the cap, no machine; not a raw import), catalysts (unconsumed inputs: circuits, moulds, lenses) and depth 12; a pin on a boundary node is warned as unused. Oredict (`ore:<name>`, D142 (h)) and wildcard (`@32767`) slots expand to their first 4 members, GregTech's first. EU is the pseudo-item `eu` (`eut x duration` per craft). Loops are kept and listed as strongly connected sets. Same request, same DB, same canonical JSON and digest; a request's `tier_cap` given as a GT name is stored as its index. **Timing** on the dev runtime's harness DB (run 20261106T014148Z, 95,125 enabled recipes): platline at IV 0.33 s (59 items, 34 recipes); nitric acid at HV 31 items, sulfuric acid at MV 15, epoxid at EV 129 (166 before the final check, with naquadah's line in it); the CI assert is 2 s. | — | WO-3.3 PR |

| D191 | 2026-11-11 | A fluid in several input slots: GT counts it slot by slot (corrects D174's Supply wording; WO-3.3) | D174 and `gtmath.parallel.Supply`'s docstring said to merge a fluid that fills several input slots into one supply. GT doesn't: `GTRecipe.maxParallelCalculatedByInputs` pools the machine's fluids, then walks the recipe's fluid slots in order, each taking `amount x min(parallels so far, pool / amount)` (two 100 L slots of one fluid, 1000 L held, max parallel 8: 2 parallels; one merged supply: 5). The docstring now says so (items are still merged: GT merges item inputs). The planner doesn't model the per-slot walk: the graph builder rejects such a recipe with the reason "fills N input slots ... (D191)", so it never overstates parallels. The dev runtime's run has no enabled, non-fake recipe of this shape (0 of 95,125), so nothing is lost today; if a pack adds one, model the walk in gtmath then. `RecipeNode.supplies(crafts)` gives gtmath one Supply per input key, an unconsumed input (a circuit) as `Supply(0, 1)` (held), never `Supply(0, 0)`, which GT reads as missing. | Corrects D174's Supply wording | WO-3.3 PR |
| D192 | 2026-11-11 | The staged MILP (WO-3.4, plan §7.1 steps 4 and 6, [E§3.4]) | **Pending reviewer (PR question).** `gregtec.planner.solve` on SciPy's `scipy.optimize.milp` (HiGHS with SciPy's defaults: its MIP search is serial, and SciPy has no thread option to pin, so determinism is tested within one process; SciPy 1.18 and NumPy are BSD-3-Clause, a core dependency now, `scipy>=1.16`). **Variables** `x_r` per recipe, `imp`/`exp` per item, gate binaries, choice binaries; **rows** conservation per item, each divided by its largest amount per craft (PlanNH's row scaling), and each recipe's column by its own largest port, so tiny rates stay above HiGHS's tolerances; the target rate is normalised to 1 while solving and its row is "at least the rate". **Free crossings:** an item nothing in the route makes is imported freely, one nothing uses is exported freely, and so is any graph boundary (raw, stock, line outputs, request, locked, depth, `eu`) both ways; every other crossing is a gate (`imp + exp <= M g`, M = 1e4, then 1e5 if a stage finds nothing). **EU** is the `eu` pseudo-item (EU/t x ticks per craft), imported freely, left out of the raw-input tiebreak and out of the plan's boundary (its power numbers are WO-3.2's sizing, overclocks included). **Stages:** (1) min gates; (2) min gated quantity + 1e-6 x raw imports (row units; no per-item cost values; review LOW-5: in row units a gate's quantity is divided by its item's largest amount per craft, so a 1000 L fluid gate weighs about 1/1000 of a one-dust gate: an implicit but structural weight, the same as PlanNH's, and it only splits ties left by stage 1's gate count); (3) min machine-seconds; (4) every recipe of the route floored at 1e-3 x the smallest running rate (crafts/s), at least 1e-6 row units, and stages 1-3 again. **HiGHS's integrality tolerance lets `M g` leak** (with M = 1e4, about 1e-2 row units through a "closed" gate): a gate whose item crosses above 1e-7 counts as open, stage 2 keeps stage 1's count plus one gate at a time until it is feasible, and stage 3 runs with stage 2's gates fixed. A recipe below 1e-7 (row units, target 1/s) is idle. **Stdout** (review HIGH-2): SciPy 1.18's HiGHS prints MIP repair lines straight to the C stdout past `disp=False`, so each `milp` call runs with fd 1 on the null device (under a lock, C stdio flushed before it is restored); `gos plan --json` stays pure JSON lines, and the harness G3 sweep stays as a numerics regression test. **Fixtures:** PlanNH (MIT, Copyright (c) 2026 Sbancuz) is MIT-compatible, so `light_fuel_hydrogen_loop.yaml` and `230_platline.yaml` (gtnh-flow charts, MIT, OrderedSet86) are copied unchanged into `tests/fixtures/plannh/` with `NOTICE.txt` (both MIT notices, PlanNH's and gtnh-flow's, commit 6506cdc0); a 30-line reader in the test parses their YAML subset (no PyYAML). Results: light-fuel loop 0 gates (CR 0.0167, electrolyser 0.015, distillery 1, as [E§3.3]); platline 1 gate, every machine running (stages 1-3 alone: 1 gate with 27 idle). | — | WO-3.4 PR |
| D193 | 2026-11-11 | The machine each recipe is sized with (WO-3.4, plan §7.1 step 5, D11, D12, D142 (a) (b), D175) | **Pending reviewer (PR question).** `gregtec.planner.sizing.pick` takes one of WO-3.2's options per recipe, for the same player the graph checked (`graph.player`: tier cap, owned, constraints, one or two plain hatches of the cap): base machines only for the base column, an addon machine first for the addon column; then unbadged before badged; a single block, then a multiblock, then a steam machine (simplest at the tier, D11); the highest tier (most overclocks, fewest machines); fewer hatches (one runs a multi at 1 A, D175); then the mte id. Counts are `ceil(rate / crafts per second of one machine)` (= `rate x ticks_eff / (20 x parallel)`), rounded to 9 digits first so float noise never adds a machine; utilisation, EU/t per machine, average (all machines over time) and peak (all running) EU/t and amps per machine go in the node. A machine with a state variable carries the worst case (the count) and the steady state (`steady`, D142 (b)); a badged machine keeps its badges and notes in the node and the plan's warnings (D142 (a)). Crafting-table and furnace recipes have no machine (count null). Swapping the machine by hand is WO-3.7's. | — | WO-3.4 PR |

Choices the sketch leaves open: **`run` is the export run key** (`20261111T134616Z`), not `export_run.id` (ids change on every `gos db build`; WO-1.9 keys on the run key); **pins name the item's stable key**, not an `n` id (`n` ids are renumbered on every solve: nodes in order of the depth of what they make, then recipe key); `id` is `pl-` + 10 hex of sha256(request, run, column, choices) until WO-3.7 persists plans and may number them; `metrics`, `rank` and `layout` are `{}` (WO-3.5, WO-3.6); edges split each item between its makers in proportion to their share of its supply; floats are written to 9 significant digits, keys sorted, no spaces, so the same request on the same DB is byte-identical (tested 12 times on the platline). | D195 | 2026-11-11 | Routes, pins, the addon column and batch mode (WO-3.4, plan §7.1 steps 3 and 6, D11, D12, D14, D61) | **Pending reviewer (PR question).** **Choices:** a primary item (WO-3.3's `primary`) with two or more chosen recipes is one choice (exactly one runs); other items may use all their chosen recipes. The choice MILP runs on the whole graph, where an unchosen branch still counts as a user of what it takes and HiGHS may let an unchosen recipe leak, so it only **proposes** a choice; each proposal is solved exactly on the recipes it reaches from the target (`solve.active`), stage 4 included, it keeps only its **effective** choices (review HIGH-1: a choice is dropped when the route doesn't run its recipe and leaving it out gives the same recipes, e.g. the choice for an input of a target recipe the route didn't pick), a no-good cut on those excludes it and every assignment of the choices it doesn't reach, and the MILP runs again; a route with the same recipes as an earlier one is dropped. The search runs until `routes + 2` routes run every machine (at most 3x that many proposals); when any does, a route whose every-machine-runs replay failed is dropped (step 4), and the best `routes` are kept, ordered by (gates, gated quantity, machines, choices). That order is fixed but is no ranking (WO-3.5 ranks). **Pins** (D61) are fixed choices: the request carries them, the graph is rebuilt with them and the design re-runs; a pinned primary item leaves its choice; a pin the column can't run (an addon-only recipe in the base column) stays in `pins` and adds a plan warning that the item is imported or made another way (review LOW-4); `alternatives.node_alternatives` lists any node's candidates (`show_all` adds the unchecked and rejected); pinning a non-primary node changed only its branch on every case tried (3 platline nodes on the harness DB). **Addon column** (D12): with `base+addons` the base routes drop recipes only addon machines run; one more route is solved with addon machines first, and is shown only when it uses at least one, each node listing its machine's lowest fact confidence. **Batch** (D14): the rate solve runs at qty per `within` (one hour by default); nodes get `crafts` for the batch, boundary ports `qty`, the target `eta_s` = the slowest node's crafts over its machines' speed (no pipeline fill time). `exact` replaces ceil by an integer MILP over machine counts under the route's gates, gated quantity and floor. CLI: `gos plan TARGET [--tier] [--qty | --batch --within] [--routes] [--addons] [--exact] [--pin ITEM=RECIPE] [--json] [--out DIR]`, `--alternatives ITEM [--all]`. | — | WO-3.4 PR |
| D203 | 2026-11-11 | Text-to-speech engines, models and licences (WO-5.3) | `voice/gregtec_voice/tts.py`; its `create(settings)` fills D179's `tts` hook. **Engines** (`[voice] tts`, Appendix C's default `kokoro`; `tts_device` `cpu` (default) or `gpu`; new `tts_voice`, "" = the engine's default): **Kokoro-82M v1.0** (weights Apache-2.0) through **kokoro-onnx 0.6.1** (MIT), voice `am_michael`, the fp32 `kokoro-v1.0.onnx` and `voices-v1.0.bin` from kokoro-onnx's `model-files-v1.0` GitHub release at pinned SHA-256s (int8 was no faster on this CPU); **Piper**: the voice `en_US-joe-medium` (rhasspy/piper-voices pinned at commit cf910fb; data CC0, chosen over lessac/amy/ryan/hfc whose data licences are non-commercial or unclear) run by GOS's own code on ONNX Runtime, re-implemented from the voice's `.onnx.json` (espeak IPA, the voice's vowel clusters as one symbol, `phoneme_id_map` ids with pads, start and end ids, the noise/length/noise-w scales): the **piper-tts package is not used**, since 1.8.0 is the GPL-3.0 piper1-gpl fork that also bundles espeak-ng; **ElevenLabs Flash v2.5** (optional, paid): plain HTTPS streaming (`/v1/text-to-speech/{voice}/stream?output_format=pcm_24000`, stdlib urllib, no SDK), the key in the keyring (`gos key set elevenlabs`; `elevenlabs` joins `gregtec.llm.keys.NAMES`), a premade voice id (Adam) until Clayton picks one; it sends answer text off the PC, so it is never a default or a fallback target. **GPU**: ONNX Runtime's CUDA or DirectML provider when installed (`onnxruntime.preload_dlls()` first); a session that silently fell back to the CPU counts as a failure. The GPU builds replace the `onnxruntime` package, so they are a PC measurement in a throwaway clone (docs/runbooks/tts.md); shipping the GPU in the release is a follow-up if the PC run needs it, so a GPU latency pick cannot be set in the release until that follow-up lands. **Fallback** (plan 0.1 principle 9): the configured engine, then it on the CPU, then Piper, then Kokoro on the CPU (`choices`); an engine that won't load is dropped; one that fails on a sentence hands the rest of that utterance to the next, and the next utterance tries it again, so one CUDA out-of-memory beside GTNH or one network blip passes; only 3 failures in a row (`STRIKES`, reset by a sentence said) drop it for the session, logged with the engine it fell back to (review M3); none at all gives a speaker that says nothing and logs why, so turns still end. Models download once to `GOS_HOME/models/{kokoro,piper}` (D185's pinned-hash `fetch`), never committed. **Dependencies**: a new non-default `tts` group (`kokoro-onnx>=0.6.1`), added to `gos deploy`'s GROUPS; CI and dev clones stay light. **Licences**: kokoro-onnx, ONNX Runtime, espeakng-loader's wrapper MIT; Kokoro weights Apache-2.0; joe CC0; but kokoro-onnx depends on **phonemizer 3.6 (GPL-3.0)** and **espeakng-loader ships espeak-ng (GPL-3.0)**, which Piper's re-implementation also uses, and more closely: GOS's Piper runner imports phonemizer's `EspeakBackend` directly rather than only through kokoro-onnx: both are installed from PyPI as libraries, never vendored or modified, no GPL source is in the repo (piper1-gpl's source was read for behaviour only), and nothing is distributed but the repo (a PR question: accept these GPL runtime dependencies, or a follow-up replaces espeak-ng with an Apache-2.0 G2P such as misaki's lexicon without its espeak fallback) | — | WO-5.3 |
| D204 | 2026-11-11 | The speech filter, the lexicon, sentence streaming, the bench and the check (WO-5.3, plan 7.15, QA:V1) | **Filter** (`speech_filter.spoken`, applied by the speaker to every reply): markup out; then **every citation and id** (plan 0.1's `r:` `q:` `mte:` `crop:` `plan:` `w:...@rev`, item keys and registry names (a namespace that starts with a letter, so ratios and times such as 3:1 and 12:30 stay numbers) including NEI's `mod:name:meta`, fluid keys, dotted and snake_case names, hex hashes, UUIDs, "id/meta N") together with the words and brackets that only pointed at them ("see r:... and q:...", "(...)"); then **D23**: at most 2 sentences and 40 words (a title such as Mr. or Dr. doesn't end a sentence), counted at this point (before pronunciations and numbers are spelt out, so "L U V" counts as the one word LuV), whole sentences first, else the first cut at its last comma (>= 8 words) or at word 40; then the **lexicon** (glossary.tsv `say`, live vault first: a term with a capital matches as written, a lower-case term in any case, whole words, plural s; a `say` that isn't plain letters, spaces, hyphens and apostrophes is ignored, since agents may write the glossary); then **numbers and units in words** (a plain four-digit 1000-9999 read in pairs as players say voltages: 1920 "nineteen-twenty", 2048 "twenty forty-eight", 1500 "fifteen hundred", except x000 and x00-x09; US style without "and"; decimals digit by digit; k/M/B/G magnitudes, but a capital K after a number is kelvin unless EU, RF, L or mB follows (10K EU is ten thousand); digits after letters are a word of their own (T1 "T one", IC2 "I C two", Mk2 "Mk two"); 3:1 "three to one", 12:30 "twelve thirty"; a spaced A before a word is a letter ("4 A circuits"), a glued or final one amps; m after a number at a clause end is minutes; EU/t, A, L, mB, s, t, min, h, %, K, °C with singular for 1; ordinals, ranges, x multipliers, GT wire sizes "16x Copper Wire" kept as "sixteen x", fractions, $, #); then caps words of 2-5 letters spelt; finally **only letters, spaces and . , ! ? ' - ; leave**, so no digit, colon, slash or underscore can reach an engine whatever slipped through. Tests: the 40 rows of `tests/evals/tts_phrases.tsv` (30 GTNH terms, 10 number/unit phrases, each with its exact expected `say`), unit tables, and a property test over 2,000 generated answers whose ids carry only the digit 7 while the answers' own words and numbers have none, so a spoken id digit shows as "seven" (no id token, no "seven", no digit, only the allowed characters, <= 40 words; a mutation that keeps "id N" fails it) plus parity with the fast lane's own REF/KEY patterns. **Streaming**: chunks are sentences, the first cut at its first comma once it has 6 words ([D§2.7]); one thread synthesises up to 2 chunks ahead of the player; audio goes out in 40 ms blocks. **`gos voice tts-bench`**: each engine through the whole speaker on ten placeholder answers (citations and numbers in, no game data), time from `speak()` to the first block handed to the device, P50 vs 400 ms; the **pick** is the cheapest that meets it in the order Piper CPU, Kokoro CPU, Piper GPU, Kokoro GPU, ElevenLabs (CPU before VRAM next to GTNH [D§2.8], the smaller model first, the paid cloud last); VRAM delta from nvidia-smi where there is one; a TSV report. The pick is printed as the **latency pick** with its gos.toml lines and the note that latency alone does not decide (Kokoro on this CPU, 1.3-3.5 s to first audio, would also break PM14's 1.7 s); Appendix C's default stays `kokoro` until Clayton's PC run and listening check (the bench measures latency only). **Results here** (4 shared vCPUs, fake device): Piper P50 136 ms / P90 221 ms (an earlier run under heavier load: 404/576 ms); Kokoro CPU P50 1,303 ms / P90 2,419 ms (earlier 3,127/6,271 ms, matching [D§2.3]'s ~3.5 s; after the review fixes, with two other agents on the CPU: Piper 246/412 ms, Kokoro 3,392/6,226 ms); no GPU here. **`gos voice tts-check`** (round trip through WO-5.2's STT, Whisper small.en on the CPU with glossary hotwords): Kokoro 39 of 40 (30/30 terms, 9/10 numbers: "thirty-two E U per tick" heard as "per tech"); Piper 31 of 40, 32 after the review fixes (letter confusions such as H V/HB, E V/TV, Z P M/CPM; "nah-kwa-dah" as "not quad out"). So on this CPU the latency pick (Piper) is the less intelligible voice: the PC decides with both the bench and `gos voice say --phrases` (the listening acceptance). `gos voice say [TEXT|--phrases]` speaks through the configured engine and device | — | WO-5.3 |
| D205 | 2026-11-11 | Output device, barge-in and Windows ducking (WO-5.3, D56, [D§2.6]) | **Device** (`playback.py`): WASAPI shared mode through sounddevice, mono int16 at the engine's rate with `auto_convert`, opened per utterance on the device PortAudio lists; PortAudio's list is made when it starts and refreshed only when capture restarts (`audio._rescan`), so a headset that comes back is found after capture restarts (it does when the same headset's microphone went away) or the client restarts; until then speech plays on the default device or fails and is logged (a write to a stream that capture's restart closed ends that utterance, never the client) (review L2). `[voice] output_endpoint` (Appendix C, default `Chat`, D56): the output device whose name contains it, any case; of several, the one whose whole name is it (SteelSeries Sonar can add a second "...Chat" device); "" = Windows' default. **No match, or several and none exact, fall back to the default device with a warning** rather than staying silent (principle 9; the conservative reading of D56, since the Arctis's Windows endpoint names are unverified); `gos voice devices` now lists output devices, marks the one used and says why. A key is checked against the catalog when written (it must name exactly one item or fluid; it is stored as the catalog spells it); a key a later pack drops stays listed, shown as not in the current DB. | D167 | 2026-11-11 | The setup wizard: steps, gos.toml and setup done (WO-2.9, plan 7.14 rule 8) | **Pending Clayton (PR question).** (1) **Registry.** `gregtec.setup.registry`: a step is a `Step` (id, title, `order`, intro, fields, a check) listed in `STEPS` of any module in `gregtec/setup/steps/`; the registry imports them all and sorts by `order` (built-in: 10 paths, 20 Anthropic key, 30 Discord token, 40 headset, 50 talk key, 60 wake phrase, 90 checklist), so a later phase adds a file and edits nothing else. A field is a gos.toml value (`path`, `text`, `choice`, `device`, `key`, `phrase`: its `(table, key)`), a keyring `secret` (with an optional test call) or the `checks` list; the dashboard draws every step from its description, so a later step needs no dashboard code either. (2) **gos.toml writes** (`gregtec.setup.tomledit`): line edits that keep Clayton's comments and every other key (a replaced key keeps its trailing comment; a missing key goes after its table's last key; a missing table at the end); refused, nothing written ("edit by hand"), when the result wouldn't read back exactly (dotted keys, inline tables, multi-line values, a file that isn't valid TOML) or would read back as anything but the old file with only those keys changed (e.g. a `key = ` line inside a multi-line string). The new text is written beside the file, read back through `config.load` (and, for `[voice]`, the voice client's own parser) and only then swapped in with `os.replace` (retried 3 times on Windows' sharing error); the old file is kept as `gos.toml.bak`. No new dependency (tomlkit was the alternative). The file is the one the core read (`--config`, else `config.default_path()`). (3) **Checks before saving:** each field by kind (required, full paths, choices), then the step's own: paths through `gos doctor`'s checks (new `doctor.checklist`: Prism folders, Play/Export/Lab there and GTNH 2.9.0, GOS_APP, GOS_HOME writable or creatable (tried with a temp file made and deleted, since `os.access` ignores Windows ACLs), the DB's GT pin; read only, mods not hashed); a missing Play is an error, missing Export, Lab or GOS_APP a warning, a GT pin mismatch a warning (plan 0.4: the recipe data is stale). An error blocks the save, a warning doesn't. **Play is never moved by a click** (review H1; final check round 2): any change of Play's folder (resolved, case ignored) is an error, "edit gos.toml by hand", once gos.toml names Play (`[paths] play` or `prism_instances`, or setup is done) or once the Play GOS runs with is a folder there: gos.toml's (defaults for what it lacks) or the one the core started with, defaults included. Only a first run whose default Play names no folder chooses Play in the wizard. Export or Lab in or around any of those Plays (gos.toml's, the core's, the one entered; symlinks and junctions resolved) is an error, so no sequence of saves turns the old Play into Export or Lab (this held only without hand edits of gos.toml; final check round 4 found it fails once one is in the sequence: superseded by D223, which records every Play). Instance names ending in a dot or a space are refused (Windows drops them, so "GTNH-Play." is GTNH-Play). GOS_HOME or GOS_APP inside a Play folder, or holding it, is an error too (final check, MEDIUM), and so is either one anywhere in Prism's instances folder (a sync of Export or Lab deletes what its source lacks; final check round 2). `doctor.checklist` makes those rows errors without probing, so a check never makes a file in Play or another instance (the GOS_HOME probe of L4 writes only outside them, and the DB's GT pin isn't even opened read-only then, since SQLite leaves -wal/-shm files beside a WAL DB: final check round 3). (4) **Setup done** is `[setup] completed_utc` in gos.toml, written by Finish only when the checklist (doctor's rows plus the keyring, which secrets are stored and `[voice]` read back) has no error row; the checklist runs the paths step's Export/Lab guard on what gos.toml says, whatever wrote it: each name must be a folder name (no path, no trailing dot or space), the three names different, and Export and Lab (resolved, case-folded, links and junctions followed) clear of every Play (gos.toml's and the running core's) and of each other, so a hand-edited `lab = "../instances/GTNH-Play"` or a saved Lab name that later became a link to Play fails Finish (final check round 3); a missing Anthropic key or Discord token, or an unavailable keyring, is a warning (no-AI mode works without them). **Only a first run (no gos.toml) opens the dashboard on the wizard alone** (review H2); once a step has written gos.toml the wizard has a "Later" button. An existing install whose gos.toml has no `[setup]` gets a dismissable "Setup isn't finished" banner above every view instead, so a deploy never locks the dashboard. Settings links to `#/setup`, which edits the same steps. (5) **Takes effect** at GOS's next start (the core read gos.toml when it started), as the wizard says; secrets work at once (the core is told, D164's key check). No restart button. (6) **Who:** `/api/setup...` takes the admin token or a paired device only (D117: client tokens get 403, unpaired callers 401; Host/Origin checks and JSON-only bodies as on every route). The first-run board (D92) came without the canvas handoff, so the view is built to `docs/design/system/` (D158 (5)): **needs Clayton's check against his approved board** | Carries out plan 7.14 rule 8 and §8 WO-2.9; adds `[setup] completed_utc` and wizard writes of `[voice] input_device`, `output_endpoint`, `talk_key`, `talk_mode` and `wake_phrase` (Appendix C) | WO-2.9 (branch `wo/2.9-setup-wizard`) |
| D168 | 2026-11-11 | Wizard secrets, key test and devices (WO-2.9) | **Pending Clayton (PR question).** (1) **Secrets:** the Anthropic key and the Discord bot token (new `keys.DISCORD`; `gos key set discord` works too) go through `gregtec.llm.keys` into the keyring (Credential Manager) after a loose shape check (`sk-ant-`, not an Admin key; a three-part bot token without "Bot "); never into gos.toml, a log line or a reply: `GET /api/setup` says only whether each is stored, the page clears the field after Store, and the tests check that the page, the API, gos.toml and the logs never hold them. (2) **Test call:** one Messages API call through the fast lane's own client (`fast_lane.client_for`, same timeouts) on its model and request shape (`claude-haiku-5-5`, thinking disabled, effort low, `max_tokens` 8), metered in `llm_usage` as `setup-test` (a fraction of a cent). Chosen over the free `models.list` probe because only a model call shows credits and model access. Refused while the AI is off by his switch or the D48 credit pause (nothing spends while off, D164); a failure feeds no-AI mode (`AiMode.failed`), a success clears it. Tests use a fake client. (3) **Devices:** `GET /api/setup/devices` lists capture and playback devices from a pluggable provider: WASAPI through `sounddevice` on Windows (PortAudio restarted first, so a headset plugged in since shows; one restart per listing for both lists, under a process-wide lock, since PortAudio isn't thread-safe), a fixed fake list elsewhere, marked fake. The voice client will enumerate them itself later. gos.toml keeps a part of a device name: `[voice] input_device` (D181; empty is Windows' default; a name matching more than one device is an error, as the voice client refuses it) and `[voice] output_endpoint` (Appendix C; default "Chat", D56; always a name, no "Windows' default" choice) | Refines plan 4.6 (secrets), D164 (a new key's check), Appendix C `[voice]` | WO-2.9 |
| D169 | 2026-11-11 | Talk key and wake phrase in the wizard (WO-2.9, QA:V8, PM08) | **Pending Clayton (PR question).** The talk key and mode are checked by the voice client's own parser (`gregtec_voice.settings.parse`, D180): F13-F24, Pause or Scroll Lock, different from the confirm, cancel and next-card hotkeys (a refusal shows on the field it is about; a clash among keys the step doesn't show is an error row, "edit gos.toml by hand"). The step also reads Play's `options.txt` once through `safe_open`, exactly as `gos voice keys play` does, and warns when GTNH binds the talk key there (or the file isn't there yet); it never writes there. Discord's keybinds are checked by hand. The wake phrase is optional: two to five words of letters, never "GregTech"/"Gregtec" in any spelling or spacing (PM08, refused), with a warning for words of GTNH talk ("computer", tier names, [H§1.3]); the suggestions are [H§1.3]'s examples ("hey archivist", "okay tessera"). WO-5.4 picks the phrase after its false-trigger tests, so the saved value is Clayton's choice until then | Refines PM08 and D180 (wizard checks) | WO-2.9 |
| D223 | 2026-11-11 | Play safety at the root: one gate before every GOS file, a record of every Play, sync refuses a former Play (WO-2.9 final check round 4) | **Pending Clayton (PR question).** (1) **One gate.** `doctor.touch_problem(path, folders)` is in front of every file of GOS's own that the wizard and `gos doctor` open, probe or create: it refuses `path` when it or its parent resolves (links and junctions followed, case-folded, a link loop counting as an overlap instead of raising) into any guarded folder, or when `path` holds one. The guarded folders (`doctor.guarded`) are every Play (gos.toml's, the running core's and every former Play), Prism's instances folder, Export and Lab. It gates the GOS_HOME write probe, the DB's GT pin, GOS_APP, gos.toml and the record file the wizard writes, and in `gos doctor` the DB read and `env.json` (against each surveyed instances folder). GOS_HOME's rows check GOS_HOME and its `data`, `logs`, `vault`, `backups` and `run` folders, resolved (`doctor.home_problem`), so a `data` folder linked into Play is an error both as a field problem and as a checklist row, and nothing is probed there. The GT pin's read-only open is now `immutable=1` (`access.connect(..., immutable=True)`), so SQLite never makes -wal/-shm files; it may miss a write still in the WAL, which is fine for a pin check and is why other read-only opens keep the plain mode. (2) **Every Play remembered.** The record is the union of `[setup] former_plays` in gos.toml and `former_plays.txt` beside gos.toml (one resolved folder per line, `#` comments), read by `config.load` into `Config.former_plays` (also when gos.toml is missing, so a deleted gos.toml keeps it). Chosen: both, because gos.toml's list survives a hand edit of `[paths]` and is visible where Clayton edits settings, and the file survives a deleted or rewritten gos.toml; neither alone covers both. The wizard adds gos.toml's Play and the running core's Play at every save and Finish (to both) and at every check once gos.toml exists (to the file only: a first-run check makes no file), and a save records the Play it is about to write first. Final checks round 5: the Play GOS runs or syncs with is recorded without the wizard too (`setup.service.record_plays`, the same guarded write): at every core start (a failure is logged; the wizard's saves and sync refuse on their own), at the dashboard's `GET /api/setup`, and by `gos sync`/`sync_instances.py` before it writes (not on a dry run; a record it can't write is a refusal), so a Play set only by hand between restarts is on record. An install from before WO-2.9 records its Play at the new version's first start; a hand edit of Play made before that first start can't be known. `gos doctor` records nothing: it reads no gos.toml, so it has no Play to record. It never removes one. `paths.plays()` includes the record, so the paths step and Finish refuse Export or Lab at any former Play after a hand edit, a hand swap, a restart or a deleted gos.toml. Former Plays don't fix Play itself: a fresh gos.toml may still choose a Play on a first run. (3) **At the point of damage.** `sync.plan_all` and `sync.plan_lab_to_export` refuse a Lab or Export target in or around a former Play, with a message that names both places to clear by hand (runbook `setup_wizard.md`, Former Plays); a record that can't be read stops sync (exit 2). Sync stays read-only on Play. Case-folded names on every OS (the round-4 low): on a case-sensitive file system a folder that differs from Play only in case is refused too, erring on the safe side. (4) **Round 6: normalised at the boundary, guarded before every write.** Every path the setup, record and sync-guard code records or compares is made absolute and resolved first, never skipped for being relative or oddly spelled (a Windows `/Games/...` has no drive, so `is_absolute()` is False, yet it is a real folder): `former_plays.txt` gets each Play resolved, and two spellings are one entry only where the file system says so (`os.path.normcase`: case ignored on Windows alone, so a distinct-case folder on a case-keeping file system stays on record). `config.load` still accepts such a `[paths]` value (a Linux CI reads Windows-spelled gos.toml files; every use resolves it). `sync._former_play` compares case-folded on every OS, exactly as `doctor.within`. Each wizard write (gos.toml, `former_plays.txt` and any folder made for them) is guarded before it is made against the current config, the record, the running core's Play, the values being saved (the candidate config) and the Play the save makes; the paths step and the checklist show an error row (`paths.config`) when gos.toml itself lies in or around any guarded folder, the candidate's included. `gos export run` (and the pack-update playbook's Play export) records Play before it plans Export, as `gos sync` does. Sync's refusal reads "Refused, nothing written in any instance" (the record may have been added to). **Threat model (Pending Clayton, PR question):** the guards are meant to hold against (i) any sequence of wizard and API calls and (ii) hand edits of gos.toml that the wizard's own texts or the runbooks suggest, with any path spelling Windows accepts. Deliberately hostile setups beyond that (for example hand-made links plus hand edits plus deleting the record) are hardening, fixed when cheap. Not done, as hardening: a file lock between processes on the record (the wizard and `gos sync` each read, merge and rewrite it under their own in-process lock; a race in that millisecond window can drop an entry only one of them knew of, and the next core start or sync records the current Play again); a warning when Lab or Export holds a link inside (sync already refuses every link it would traverse) **Round 7:** every write of `former_plays.txt` merges the lines already in it, so a write never has fewer entries than the file had, and while gos.toml can't be read the wizard counts the record beside it with the running core's config (as it does when gos.toml is missing); a record that can't be read is refused (409), never overwritten. | Corrects D167's over-claim; adds `[setup] former_plays` and `former_plays.txt` (Appendix C) | WO-2.9 (branch `wo/2.9-setup-wizard`) |
| D206 | 2026-11-11 | Persona packs: where, what, the never-rules and the active persona (WO-5.6, plan 7.11, D28, D60, PM09) | **Where**: the shipped packs (`gt-veteran`, the default by D60, `ship-ai`, `coop-buddy`) and `_template.md` are authored in the repo's `vault-template/_gos/personas/pe-<name>.md` and deployed with `_gos/` (`gos deploy` overwrites them; never edited in the live vault, as CLAUDE.md says of skills); the core reads them from the repo's template first, else the live vault's `_gos/personas/`. Clayton's own packs are the live vault's `personas/pe-<name>.md`; one with a shipped pack's name replaces it, which is how he changes a shipped one. Agents only draft (plan 6.5: "agents draft on request"): the vault guard (`vault.io.write_refusal`, so `note_write`, `note_append` and the SDK's Write/Edit through the `PreToolUse` hook) refuses agent writes anywhere in `personas/` except `personas/drafts/`, which the loader never reads (it reads one folder, not its subfolders); Clayton makes a draft live by moving it up a folder. So no agent, and no wiki, quest or chat text an agent reads, can change a pack that reaches the prompts (review H1). The shipped ones sit under `_gos/` so a deploy can update them without touching Clayton's files; plan 6.1 and Appendix B say so. `_gos/personas/` joins `NOT_NOTES` (not in INDEX.md or the curator). **Format** (a `t: persona` note in the plan 6.2 grammar, its keys now in `_gos/schema.md`): `name` (display name), `voice` (one `engine:voice` entry per engine: `kokoro:`, `eleven:`, `piper:`, D207), `verbosity` (terse, short or chatty), `humour`, `address` (how to address Clayton), `style` (one rule a line, at least one), `lexicon`, `sample` (5 original lines; more are cut to 5), `note` (never reaches a prompt). A line that breaks a rule is left out and reported (`gos persona`, the Settings view), the rest loads: an unknown key (`rules:`, `tools:`, ...), a digit in any text that reaches a prompt (a persona never brings a number: plan 0.1), `<` or `>` (it can't close the block it is quoted in), a voice that isn't `engine:name`. The display name is at most 40 letters, spaces, `-` and `'` (else the pack doesn't load) and appears only inside the quoted block, never in the preamble (review M3). **Never-rules** (`personas.NEVER_RULES`: numbers from tools only with their citations, world actions need Clayton's confirmation, spoilers nudge-first under the one global setting, tone only) are code, not pack text: the pack is quoted as data inside `<persona>` and the rules always follow it, so no pack can remove, reorder or replace them (tested with a hostile pack). **Active persona**: at start a random pack when `[persona] random_per_session` is on, else the one last chosen (`GOS_HOME/data/persona.json`), else `[persona] default` (Appendix C), else `gt-veteran`; a default that isn't a pack falls back with a warning. Packs are read again whenever a pack file is added, removed or changed (a signature of the folders' listings and the files' sizes and modification times, a few `stat` calls per use), so a new pack made from `_template.md` loads with no code change or restart. When `random_per_session` is on, the session's pick isn't saved; a later explicit switch is (and counts only until the next start picks at random again). **Switching**: the instant command "persona <name>" / "switch to the <name> persona" / "persona random" / "which persona" through WO-2.3's router (the first instant rule; no model call, works in no-AI mode; the STT's punctuation after "persona" is accepted, e.g. "Persona, Ship AI."; a "name" of more than 3 words, one with words no pack name holds (`for`, `of`, `recipe`, ...) or a deep-lane request falls through to the other rules), `POST /api/persona` (admin, a paired device, or the client token named `discord` for WO-9.x's `/persona`, as for the credit decision), the Settings dropdown (which calls it), and `gos persona [NAME]` (lists packs and problems; switches through the running core). A name matches the pack's name or display name, spoken or typed ("Ship AI", "co-op buddy", "shipai"). A switch is kept for the next start and pushed to the dashboards with the state; every reply carries the active persona (`persona`: id, name, voice). Pack problems (file names, parse errors) go to admin and paired devices only (the trusted dashboard snapshot, `GET/POST /api/persona` as them) | — | WO-5.6 |
| D207 | 2026-11-11 | The TTS voice follows the persona (WO-5.6, D203) | Each reply's `persona.voice` (the pack's entries, e.g. `kokoro:am_fenrir, eleven:VR6AewLTigWG4xSOukaG`) is handed by the voice client to `Speaker.use_voice` before the reply is said; from the next chunk on, each engine speaks in its entry's voice, over `[voice] tts_voice`; an engine with no entry keeps its configured voice, so a pack never changes which engine runs (that stays `[voice] tts` and D203's fallback order, and a pack can't send text to the paid cloud). Kokoro switches voice on the loaded model (no reload; a `b...` voice speaks British English); ElevenLabs keeps the key and model and changes the voice id; Piper has one pinned voice (joe, D203), so a `piper:` entry changes nothing until more voices are pinned. A voice the engine lacks is logged once and the engine's own voice speaks (principle 9). Shipped voices: gt-veteran Kokoro `am_fenrir` / ElevenLabs premade Arnold; ship-ai `bf_emma` / Rachel; coop-buddy `am_puck` / Josh (premade voice ids, as D203's Adam, until Clayton picks; a listening check on his PC) | — | WO-5.6 |
| D208 | 2026-11-11 | Persona delivery to the lanes, and what WO-5.6 leaves out (WO-5.6, plan 4.4, 7.0) | **Fast lane**: the persona (the quoted pack plus the never-rules) is the last of its cached system blocks, after rules + skills and INDEX.md, each with its own `cache_control` breakpoint (3 of the API's 4), read per call: tools, rules and INDEX.md stay one byte-identical cached prefix and a switch rewrites only the tail block (tested). **Deep lane**: its system prompt is fixed for a session ([D§1.6]), so `hooks.Gate.prompt_context`, the `UserPromptSubmit` hook, adds the active persona as `additionalContext` to every prompt, read when the prompt is sent, so the next deep-lane reply after a switch uses the new persona (tested with a fake SDK client that runs the hook as the CLI does); it fails open (no persona is only a change of tone; the game-state snapshot joins it with WO-6.x). **Left out** (PR questions): plan 7.0's `persona_set` model tool and plan 4.5's `persona` subagent ("switch or create personas"): switching already works with no model through the router, the API, the dropdown and the CLI, and giving a model a state-changing tool needs a gate and a subagent definition that no WO-5.6 acceptance asks for, so `catalog.LATER` lists it as a WO-5.6 follow-up. Agents can draft a pack with `note_write`, into `personas/drafts/` only, which never goes live without Clayton moving it (D206); when `persona_set` lands it switches only to an existing pack, never to a draft. The Settings dropdown is designed to the WO-2.0 system (tokens, Panel, a native select in the control tokens) and needs Clayton's board approval (D92) | — | WO-5.6 |
| D222 | 2026-11-11 | G1's 30 golden recipes: the rule, the check and the Lab half (G1 review, plan §8 G1, §9) | **Reviewer: agree, with changes (2026-11-12; the stricter item match and the independence note below are those changes). Clayton's sign-off batched with G1 (D158 (3)).** Phase 1 left no golden set, so one is picked by a written rule (`select` in `tests/integration/test_golden_recipes.py`) from a client harness run's DB: recipes that are enabled, not hidden, not fake, not GT recycling, with an output, in a map GT shows in NEI (fuel maps left out) or in an NEI-only handler; in citation-key order (the key is a content hash, so the order is a fixed shuffle); each pick from a map or handler not picked yet while one is left. Strata: 9 GT recipes one per tier ULV–UV from GregTech's own single-block maps; 5 from GregTech maps only multiblocks run; 3 with a fluid input and a fluid output; 3 with a chanced output; 3 with an oredict input slot; 4 from maps owned by addon mods, one per mod; NEI's shaped, shapeless and furnace handlers, one each. The fixture `tests/fixtures/golden/g1_recipes.tsv` holds only keys (`r:` ref, NEI handler, first output's registry key) and names, no game number. **The check** reads NEI's JSON on its own (not WO-1.3's merge), pairs each DB slot with an NEI stack that shows or lists in `anyOf` the item the DB shows for that slot (meta 32767 is any meta; the slot's `recipe_input_alt` items and oredict members don't count, because WO-1.3's merge fills them from NEI's own `anyOf`, so pairing on them would compare NEI with itself: reviewer finding 1), and compares EU/t, duration, every amount (items and mB) and every chance; inputs GT keeps in `recipe_meta` (TecTech condensate fluids, the Beamcrafter's particles) count as slots. **How independent it is** depends on the recipe's `source`: g28–g30 (`crafting`, `furnace`) are NEI's own lines, so they test ingest fidelity only; GT-vs-NEI agreement is shown by g01–g27. For the `gt+nei` ones the merge's identity hash already includes the slot amounts, so their cross-source checks are EU/t, duration and chances; g25 (`gt` only) is checked on everything, its condensate amounts included. NBT isn't compared (NEI's `tag` has numeric ids; the key's NBT hash is WO-1.3's tested code). The test runs in CI's mod job after every client harness run (`GOS_REQUIRE_HARNESS_RUN=1`) and on any built GOS_HOME (`GOS_GOLDEN_HOME`), with a second harness test that the rule still gives 30 picks in the strata's counts from distinct maps; a full-pack DB whose keys differ uses the command line's `--reselect`, not pytest (the fixture is the dev runtime's). **The Lab half** (`docs/runbooks/g1_golden_lab.md`) compares the same 30 with NEI's pages in the game, including what the dump doesn't carry (amperage, coil heat, cleanroom, special items, additives), from `show`'s DB values, never from numbers in a doc. G1's "100%" needs both halves. | — | G1 review (`docs/reviews/G1_review.md`) |
| D196 | 2026-11-11 | The effort model (WO-3.5, plan §7.2 "Effort model", D17, D62) | **Pending reviewer (PR question).** `gregtec.planner.effort`. **Build components:** each node's machine item (`machine.item_id`) times its count, and for a multiblock the blocks of its smallest `structure` variant (WO-1.8) times its count; hatches, cables, pipes and rotors wait for the layout (WO-3.6, WO-10.5), and a multiblock with no structure row gets a note. **Making a component** runs the designer's own pipeline on it as a batch at the request's tier (WO-3.3's graph with the request's boundary, WO-3.4's machines and staged MILP), every primary choice its first, simplest recipe (D11), so loops balance as they do in a line; whole crafts per recipe, the fractional crafts summed over the whole walk first and rounded up once; a GT recipe costs crafts x the machine-seconds per craft its sized machine needs (GT's math, never estimated); a crafting-table or furnace step (NEI's, no machine and no duration in the DB) costs `craft_s` per craft (2 s, `craft_s:` in `me/prefs.md`). **An item the flow imports through a gate is made too** (review M4): the staged MILP's first objective (fewest gates) imports a whole sub-assembly through one gate when making it would open several deeper ones, so a machine's flow gates its MV hull; `Effort.make` makes each gated item the same way, in the quantity imported, down to 3 levels below the component (as unlocks) and never inside its own making. Only an item with no recipe or route at the tier, or gated deeper than that or in a loop, is not counted, and the notes list them in one note per cause (not one note per item). **Spread** per recipe map over the machines My base owns on that map, at least 1. **Unlocks:** a step on a map where he owns no machine and the route builds none needs that machine first; its item is made the same way, recursively to depth 3, each map unlocked once. **Judgement call:** with no owned machines listed (WO-3.8 not filled in yet) every map counts as one machine he has, so `unlock_h` is 0 and a note says so; `new_types` uses the same test (D198), so it is empty too. The breakdown table is `metrics.effort.rows` (part, map, machine, crafts, machines, hours). The server harness exports no NEI crafting recipes and no structures, so on the harness DB machine items can't be made and `eff_h` is 0 with notes; the model is tested on a fixture with an assembler and two shaped recipes, and on the client harness run (NEI's crafting recipes: steel at MV, with gated items made, gives 7.1-7.6 h of build effort per route (71 machines; the largest rows are macerator, crafting-table and alloy-smelter steps) with 3 notes each, one of them listing the 56 items gated deeper than 3 levels or in a loop, the MV hull costed; owning one machine type unlocks the rest). **Cost:** making the gated items runs WO-3.3's graph builder (about 1.3 s each, 93 builds for steel at MV) on every item reached, so a steel design on the client DB takes about 90 s on this 4-CPU shared container where it took 8-39 s per design before (the graph builder is about 85% of it, by cProfile); WO-3.7's re-solve on pin should cache component walks per DB run and tier (follow-up, PR question). Making a component takes WO-3.3's first-ranked recipe at every choice, which on the real DB sometimes picks an unusual path (a bacterial vat or brewer step inside a steel line's components); it is the same heuristic the lines use and applies alike to every route, so it stays for now; **follow-up** (PR question for Clayton): prefer maps the route or My base already uses when making components. | — | WO-3.5 PR |
| D197 | 2026-11-11 | Scarcity (WO-3.5, plan §7.1 step 7, D62, [QA:D10]) | **Pending reviewer (PR question).** `metrics.scarcity` = sum over the route's raw inputs of per hour x GT material units x weight, from `data/curated/scarcity.tsv` (key, weight 0-1, source, rationale). A row keys a stable item key (`registry@meta`, `fluid:<name>`) or `material:<GT material>`; an explicit key wins over its material; a material row covers every item GT tags with it, scaled by the DB's `gt_material_amount` over one unit (3628800); fluids count per 1000 L; an unlisted input weighs 0. **Judgement call:** the 26 starting rows (platinum group, naquadah, rare earths, a few isotopes and byproduct metals) are the PM's defaults, `source = pm-default 2026-11-11`: a preference, not a game number, which Clayton tunes in the file, as he tunes the scarcity weight in `me/prefs.md`. The table loader refuses a bad header, a non-key, a weight outside 0-1, empty cells and duplicates. | — | WO-3.5 PR |
| D198 | 2026-11-11 | Deterministic ranking and its weights (WO-3.5, plan §7.1 step 8, [QA:D2], D62, D67) | **Pending reviewer (PR question).** `gregtec.planner.rank`. Six metrics, lower is better: machines, new machine types (how many: a route machine is new when it runs a recipe map on which My base lists no owned machine, the effort model's unlock test, review M3; owning an LV mixer makes an MV mixer not new, its tier-up cost being in the build effort; with no owned machines listed nothing is new, §7.2's default, D196), EU/t (**average**, the steady draw; peak is reported, not ranked), effort (`eff_h`), scarcity, complexity (nodes + fluids + loops). **Normalised** by the largest value among the routes compared (0 when every route has 0), **score** = 1 - the weighted mean of the terms (1 = nothing of anything), terms and scores rounded to 9 decimals so float noise can't order routes. **Order:** score, then fewer new machine types, then the lower highest machine tier, then the plan id (a hash of the route's choices); nothing depends on dict or set order. The base routes are ranked among themselves and named for their place; the addon route (D12) is scored against the base routes and itself and keeps its own column after them. **Weights** live in the vault's `me/prefs.md`, read only (never written): `weights: balanced` (D67, every weight 1), optional `w_<metric>: <number >= 0>` lines (then the set is `custom`) and `craft_s:`; a bad value keeps its default and adds a note; every weight 0 falls back to balanced. Every result carries `rank.w` and `gos plan` prints the weights line first (`rank.describe`). Plan JSON floats in `metrics` and `rank` are rounded to 9 significant digits like the rest of the plan, so 12 re-solves stay byte-identical. | — | WO-3.5 PR |

| D246 | 2026-11-11 | Keys Discord users often bind (WO-5.5, D58, D180, QA:V8) | GOS never reads Discord's keybinds (they are Discord's settings; WO-5.5 keeps the voice client out of Discord's process, settings and files), so `gos voice keys` and `gos voice doctor` can only warn. Discord binds none of D180's keys by default; the warning names the keys people often bind by hand to Push to Talk, Push to Mute or Toggle Mute: **Pause and Scroll Lock** (a keyboard's spare keys). F13-F24 get no per-key warning (review advisor verdict): D180 picked F13-F16 because Discord binds none of them, so a warning there would fire on 3 of the 4 default keys every run and be ignored, and mouse software usually maps side buttons to XButton1/2, not F13; the single "check Discord's Settings > Keybinds" line under every key list and the runbook's check by hand cover them. Under open mic (D58) such a key would key or mute Discord's microphone with every GOS press. The warning is a line under the key (`Discord WARNING: ...`), not a failure: the exit codes stay GTNH's (`keys`) and the doctor's own checks. The list is a judgement from common setups, not a Discord source; the runbook's Settings > Keybinds check is what proves it. The research's "bind the talk key to Discord's Push To Mute too" [H§2.2] is not recommended: that is a Discord mute, which D58 drops. | — | WO-5.5 PR |
