Nothing sets Windows' default devices or PortAudio's (`sounddevice.default`); (3) the hook passes every key on: pynput's listener gets only `win32_event_filter`, never `suppress`, and the filter never calls `suppress_event`; the watchdog's missed keys go to GOS's own queue, never back into Windows' input; (4) a static audit of `voice/gregtec_voice/**/*.py` (AST; a denylist plus two allowlists, so a name built at run time can't slip past it: every function taken off a loaded DLL is one of `OpenThread`, `SetThreadPriority`, `CloseHandle`, `GetAsyncKeyState`, `GetTickCount` (ptt.py), never chained or passed to `getattr`, and `importlib.import_module` only loads `gregtec_voice.*`): no key injection (`SendInput`, `keybd_event`), no endpoint or default-device changes (`SetMute`, `SetDefaultEndpoint`, `IPolicyConfig`, volume), no other process or window (`OpenProcess`, `FindWindow`, `PostMessage`, psutil, pywin32, pypresence), no registry writes, `winreg` only in `doctor.py` (read only), `subprocess` only in the TTS bench (nvidia-smi), and no string naming Discord's files, `discord.exe` or its RPC pipe. The doctor's "Windows' default" devices are WASAPI's (the console role); the Default Communication Device would need Core Audio COM, so the runbook has Clayton note it by hand (mmsys.cpl). A ducking value that isn't a number is a `!` line, not a crash. Appendix C's `discord_auto_mute` stays `false` and ignored (D58: GOS never mutes Discord). **`gos voice doctor`** (one subcommand of `gos voice`, no framework): prints the capture mode, the microphone and output device used, Windows' default devices (never changed), Windows communications ducking (`UserDuckingPreference`, read with `KEY_READ`; anything but 3 is marked with `!` and points to WO-5.3's `set_comm_ducking.ps1`), the hook (what it does, and on Windows a moment's install and removal), the triggers and the four keys with D246's warnings. Lines that need attention start with `!` and give exit code 1; on Linux the Windows checks say `not checked: Windows only`. Discord's own input/output device, keybinds and attenuation can't be seen from GOS and are checked by hand in `docs/runbooks/discord_coexistence.md` (the 1 h session check, §8's acceptance, a PC step). | — | WO-5.5 PR |
| D248 | 2026-11-11 | `gos voice benchmark`: one harness, where it lives, what it measures, and D23 counted as heard (WO-5.7, PM14, D23, QA:A7) | **Reviewer agreed (WO-5.7 review, 2026-11-12), with the count, cut and live-turn changes marked *review* below; still a PR question for Clayton.** **One command:** `gos voice benchmark` (`gos voice bench` is its alias, so the WO-5.1-5.5 runbooks still work; WO-5.1's log-only bench is its latency section, same lines) checks four sections by code and writes a TSV (`section scope metric value target result note`) and a text summary to `GOS_HOME/data/reports` (or `--out`): PASS when every gate was measured and met, FAIL when one missed, INCOMPLETE otherwise; exit 1 on FAIL or when no turn was measured. `gos voice tts-bench` stays separate (it picks an engine, D204). Plain `bench` therefore also runs the eval set and writes a report each run (*review*: said in the runbooks that use it). **Where:** `core/gregtec/voicebench.py`, reached from `gregtec_voice.cli` before its parser: reading the core's and the voice client's memory reaches other processes, which D247's audit forbids the voice package (psutil and `OpenProcess` are on its lists); the core may. psutil 7.2 (BSD-3-Clause) is a core dependency (small, wheels for Windows), imported lazily: without it RAM is `not measured`. *Review:* the D247 audit doesn't follow imports, so it now names every core module the voice package imports and allows `gregtec.voicebench` only in `cli.main`; any other core import fails it. **Latency:** the voice client's `turn` records (D181), the last `--last` (100) or, with `--minutes M`, those logged while the command samples; per trigger each stage's P50/P90 and PM14 on the P50 of `total_ms` (key up or end of speech to first audio). **Resources:** every 1 s, RAM available system-wide (its minimum is "free at peak", gate >= 4096 MB) and the RSS (on Windows the peak working set) of the processes whose command line runs `gregtec.api` (the core) or `gos voice run` / `gregtec_voice run` (`--pid role=PID` overrides); every 5 s nvidia-smi's used/total VRAM and its compute-apps per process where WDDM reports them (L7's >= 1 GB headroom is shown, not gated: it is lab-ops'). The benchmark only reads; it starts, stops and changes nothing. **Synthetic run** (`--synthetic`, the fakes acceptance): the real `VoiceClient` and `Process` in-process with the fake hook and microphone, 20 ms blocks in real time (a 440 Hz tone for speech, zeros for silence), N talk-key turns then N wake turns; a wake engine that wakes on the tone and reports end of speech at the last loud block after `wake.END_SILENCE_S` (0.6 s) of silence, as the real one; a fixed transcript; the fast lane on the fake model over GOS_HOME's DB (a canned answer without one); a speaker running the real speech filter and lexicon; `--stt-ms/--core-ms/--tts-ms` add stage time. **Spoken length:** the 60 questions of `tests/evals/fast_lane_questions.tsv` through `Asker` on the fake model (D153, a fresh conversation each), each answer through `tts.prepare(lexicon)` (exactly what the speaker says); an answer is within D23 when it is heard as <= 2 sentences and <= 40 words (`speech_filter.said`); gate >= 95%. *Review:* that gate is met by construction on what the filter lets through, so a second gate counts the answers the filter had to cut (dropped sentences, or cut at a comma or the word limit): <= 5% (the 95% read the other way; an answer cut mid-thought may have lost its point, which the prompt rules are there to prevent). **Live turns (*review*):** each `turn` record now carries `spoken_sentences`, `spoken_words` and `spoken_cut` of the answer it spoke (counts only, D82; the speaker computes them after it starts speaking, so first audio waits for nothing), and the benchmark applies the same two gates to the measured turns as "Spoken length (live)": on the PC that is the real model's answers, at no extra cost. The eval set is never asked of the real model (that would be spending). **D23 counted as heard (changes D204's count):** D204 counted words before numbers were spelt out, so "30,720 EU/t for 1,234 ticks with 32,768 L" passed as short while it is heard as 48 words. Now step 3 counts each sentence as said (`said_words`: its numbers and units in words, step 5's own output), each word of the reply as written counting once however it is spelt ("L U V", "E U", "E B Fs": one each), so LuV and GTNH still count once as D204 meant. *Review:* a run of single capitals used to count as one word whatever its source, so "MV EBF" was 1 and the pronoun "I" merged with a following "A"; now the filter marks the reply's own word boundaries (an en space, internal to the filter, never said) before the lexicon and spelling run, and a spelt run counts at most 5 letters a word; and a new last step trims what is heard to 40 words should a pronunciation have added words. On the eval set with the fake model: 60 of 60 within and 0 of 60 cut (tools' test DB and a harness DB, after the review's count change); the prompt rules weren't changed (the fast lane's RULES already ask for one or two short sentences). The real model's answers are checked only as the live turns of the PC run (above); if more than 5% are cut there, the prompt rules change in a follow-up. **Licences:** psutil BSD-3-Clause; nothing vendored. | Refines D179 (the voice client's `bench`), D204 (D23's word count), D247 (why the benchmark is the core's); carries out plan 8 WO-5.7 with D249 | WO-5.7 PR |
| D249 | 2026-11-11 | FPS and TPS for the benchmark: from what GTNH shows, on Lab, and what counts as impact (WO-5.7) | **Reviewer agreed (WO-5.7 review, 2026-11-12), with the guards marked *review*; still a PR question for Clayton.** Nothing is added to the game: no mod hook, no injected command, no screen reading, and never Play (`docs/runbooks/voice_benchmarks.md` uses GTNH-Lab). **FPS:** F3's `N fps` readings, typed by Clayton (five, ten seconds apart, the same spot and facing) before GOS runs and while it runs; `--fps-before/--fps-during` take text or a file (numbers alone on a line, or one line of numbers and commas, also count; *review*: no other digits, so a log's timestamps are never FPS); the mean of each; gate: drop `(before - during) / before` <= 10% (plan 8). GTNH 1.7.10 logs no FPS, so there is no log line to read. **TPS:** `/forge tps` in Lab's chat (Open to LAN with cheats, that session only); `--tps-before/--tps-during` take the text or a copy of Lab's `logs/latest.log` (chat is logged as `[CHAT]`), read once through `gamestate.safe_open`; the last `Overall : Mean tick time: X ms. Mean TPS: Y` line counts, decimals with a point or a comma (Forge formats by locale). **"No TPS impact"** = the mean TPS drops by at most 0.1 (the conservative reading: /forge tps caps at 20, so a server with headroom shows no drop at all; 0.1 allows only rounding and sampling noise); the mean tick time is reported beside it, not gated (a busier tick under 50 ms is not a TPS impact). A file named but not there is `not measured` (its name's digits are never a reading). *Review:* a during reading whose last `Overall` line is the before one (the same timestamped line: the log copied twice with no new `/forge tps`, e.g. a mistyped command), or an untimestamped paste that holds the whole before text, is `not measured` ("no new /forge tps reading"), never a pass. **Out of scope here:** starting voice with Play's pre-launch hook (D179 gave it to WO-5.7): the benchmark runs `gos voice run` by hand on Lab and needs nothing from Play's hooks, and wiring the supervisor to Play's launch touches Play; **this row moves it to WO-6.1** (game watchers: it already watches Play's launch), whose plan row now says so; WO-5.7 does not do it. | Refines plan 8 WO-5.7 (how FPS and TPS are read), D179 (voice with Play's pre-launch hook moves from WO-5.7 to WO-6.1; until then `gos voice run`) | WO-5.7 PR |

No fuel map was missing from the recipe dump, so the recipe export is unchanged. **DB** (schema version 4, new tables only, no migration file): `rotor_size`, `rotor_material`, `rotor_gt`, `generator`, `generator_gt`, `fuel_container` and `fuel`, one set per export run, pruned with it. **Fuel catalog** (`core/gregtec/planner/fuels.py`, `fuel` rows written by `gos db build`): the 16 maps of plan 7.2 (`dieselFuels`, `extremeDieselFuels`, `gasTurbineFuels`, `hotFuels`, `denseLiquidFuels`, `semiFluidFuels`, `plasmaFuels`, `magicFuels`, the five naquadah reactor maps, `rocketFuels`, `rtgFuels`, `largeBoilerFakeFuels`), each fuel recipe decoded by its map's rule with unit and citation (`r:<map>/<sig12>`): EU per litre for a fluid or a fluid container (FUEL_VALUE, [E§4.1]), EU per item (FUEL_VALUE x 1000) for an item that holds no fluid, EU per pellet for RTG pellets (what GT++'s `MTERTGenerator.getFuelValue` makes at 100 %: special x 1 728 000 ticks, a float product as `convertDaysToTicks` computes it, x the recipe's EU/t, capped at `Integer.MAX_VALUE` like GT; review MEDIUM-2: NEI's `gt.nei.rtg.days` page shows special x 365 "days", which is not how long the RTG burns a pellet, so the catalog keeps the machine's reading), the steel boiler's burn ticks per 1000 L (the recipe's duration) or the furnace burn value / 1600 for large boilers. `FUEL_VALUE` is the metadata key when present, else the special value (they are aliases; the harness test checks they agree). **Generator catalog** (`core/gregtec/planner/generators.py`): every generator of the machine pass as an entry by GT class (`mte:<id>`), kind, new or legacy controller, fuel maps and sizing (`turbine`, `single_block` only for a basic generator whose map is a fuel map read as EU, else `not_modelled`, D232); client tokens read. **Board:** the Designer board is approved (D92) but its canvas handoff hasn't come through, so the view is built to `docs/design/system/` and plan 7.14 (D158 (5)) and **needs reconciling with Clayton's board** at the deployment test. It returns the text and its `source` (model, model-retry, template); the calls are metered in `llm_usage` (lane `deep`, task `plan_explain`) and sit outside the deep lane's per-task cap (D156), which prices the session's own messages only. A vault that can't take a note is a reported problem, not a failure (the row is the plan, as in WO-3.6). **Order (review M1):** accept and rename read the plan's names book (its export run) and build both note bodies before any write; a plan whose export run `gos db build` has pruned (`keep_runs`) is refused whole (`DesignerError`: "solved on an export run that's gone: re-solve it"), never half-applied. **Ledger hook:** `gregtec.planner.ledger.ledger_update(plan, status)` is the stub WO-4.4 makes real: it returns the entry WO-4.4 will record (the plan's own EU/t per tier from its metrics) with `recorded: false`, and records nothing. **Names:** `rename` (dashboard, `plan_rename`, `gos plan --rename`) sets the row's name; the JSON, exports, plan note and line note follow, and a re-solve keeps it (WO-3.6's save keeps the row's name); 1-80 characters, without square brackets, pipes or backticks. **Deferred to WO-4.4 (review: agreed; a WO-4.4 dependency):** My base's `line_outputs` (D211) still returns nothing; WO-4.4, which owns the running-lines state, feeds running lines' outputs into the designer's boundary (D63) and makes `ledger_update` real. | - | WO-3.7 PR |
| D253 | 2026-11-12 | Deep-lane cost on 10 design tasks and the `task_max_usd` rule (WO-3.7, [QA:A4], D156, D157) | **Pending Clayton (spending; PR question).** `gos eval design-cost` hands the 10 rows of `tests/evals/design_tasks.tsv` (steel, polyethylene, sulfuric acid, silicon, rubber compare, stainless, platline, epoxid batch, nitric acid with addons, a saved-plan lookup) to the deep lane one session each and reports per task the SDK's cost and the lane's own estimate (`est_usd`, what the cap is checked against), the explanation's planner-model cost (`explain_usd`, `plan_explain`, D251), turns, tools, subagents, seconds, cost by model and the mode (real or fake). It runs on a copy of GOS_HOME's DB and vault (`data/eval/design-cost-home`, replaced by each run; review L2), so its plans, notes and the Designer's latest design never reach the live ones; only `llm_usage` is live. A task reaching the measuring cap (`--measure-cap`, default the current cap) is stopped, never approved. **Rule (judgement call):** `task_max_usd` = the worst task's cost (larger of SDK and estimate) x 1.5, rounded up to $0.25, at least $0.50; a run with a capped or failed task recommends nothing. `--apply` writes it into gos.toml's `[budget]` (line edit keeping comments, `gos.toml.bak` kept; `tomledit` now writes floats), only after a real run of all 10 default tasks (refused with `--fake`, `--tasks` or `--limit`); `--apply-from <table.tsv>` applies a saved real run's table without running (and paying) again, refused unless it holds the 10 default tasks in order, all `real` (review L1). The rule prices the lane's session only (what the cap measures); `explain_usd` is reported beside it, not added. **Fake run (here):** the real Claude Code CLI against the fake API replaying `tests/evals/design_tokens.tsv` (synthetic per-turn token counts shaped like the WO-2.4 fake runs: the orchestrator calling design_production then plan_explain, whose writer call replays the planner row, then a final answer); its tools run for real on the scratch copy. On the tool tests' made-up DB only the steel targets (g01, g06, g10) can be designed; the other tasks' tools return errors to the scripted model and still end `done` (review L3: their cost is a failed design's). Result (2026-11-12, after review): worst $0.0871 (g07), mean $0.0606, median $0.0582, explanations $0.054 to $0.073 where they ran -> recommends $0.50 (labelled "replayed token counts, not a measurement"). The `[budget] task_max_usd` default stays 2.0 until Clayton's real run (runbook `docs/runbooks/designer.md`, worst case 10 x $2.00 = $20 plus a subagent's overrun past the pause, plus at most two explanation calls per task, about $0.32 each on the planner model at the most) and `--apply-from`. **Review (2026-11-12): rule agreed, L1 changes made; the acceptance item "task_max_usd set from the measurements" stays open until that run.** | Supersedes D156's "placeholder until WO-3.7 measures" once applied | WO-3.7 PR |
| D233 | 2026-11-12 | A power line, its generators, fuels and farms (WO-4.2, plan 7.2, D11, D13, D65, D232) | **Pending reviewer (PR questions).** `gregtec.planner.power`. **A line** = one generator block fixed at the build unit (a recipe of the solver's network that burns the fuel and gives EU and one `power:unit`, the target), the fuel's chain designed by the 7.1 engine (WO-3.3 graph, WO-3.4 MILP; EU never imported: `solve.Network.banned`, a one-line addition that fixes an import at 0) and distribution (output tier, voltage, amps; cables are WO-4.5). Net EU/t = what the line exports. **Chain machines** are picked for the least EU per craft (multiblocks sized with hatches of every tier up to the cap, so a recipe can run without overclocks; then the fewest machines; this departs from D11's simplest sub-step, fewest machines first, because a power line pays for every EU itself), and each recipe's EU in the network is its sized draw, not the recipe's base EU/t: every EU comes out of the line's own output. A chain recipe the export lists with no input it uses up (a few `multicentrifuge`/`centrifuge`/`fishpond` rows whose input didn't key) is left out with a note, never counted as free output (and the validator refuses one, D234). **Generators**: 2.9 controllers only (D232 (3)); `single_block`: per fuel map the highest-tier single block at the cap, and for `self_sustaining` and `output_per_effort` also the most efficient one at or below it (a lower tier often burns less per EU, advisor verdict 11) (eu_out x amps; EU per litre or item is GT's `getFuelValue`, truncated as GT truncates it, `fuels.single_block_eu`: never the exact fraction, review HIGH-1; a fuel GT gives 0 EU for is dropped with a note); `gas_turbine`/`plasma_turbine`: the large turbine at its optimal flow (WO-4.1 port) with the request's rotor (default **Steel large**: a question) on the smallest 1 A dynamo up to the cap that doesn't cap it; `boiler_steam`: a 2.9 large boiler on a fluid fuel (`gtmath.boiler`, re-implemented from `MTELargeBoilerBase` at the pin: steady state at full efficiency, one fuel kind, `runtimeBoost` in Java integer division, superheated boilers only on GT's allowed fuels) feeding a large steam turbine (HP for superheated) at its optimal flow, boilers = ceil(flow / steam per boiler), throttled with the highest programmed circuit that still gives the flow (`adjustEUtForConfig`: lEUt = max(25, EUT - 25 x circuit), 75 for the superheated boilers; `adjustBurnTimeForConfig` stretches the burn time so EU per fuel holds), fuel and water counted at that throttle for every boiler built, the steam over the flow noted; the circuit is in the kit. Solid boiler fuels are **not sized**: the export keeps a solid's furnace value only divided by 1600 (rounded down), so charcoal etc. wait for the export to carry `GTModHandler.getFuelValue` (follow-up). The boiler and tree-farm ports are checked against GT's own classes, as D231 asked (`tests/unit/gtmath/oracle/GtBoilerOracle.java` writes `vectors/boiler.tsv`, `test_boiler_vectors.py` replays it): the four 2.9 boilers' EUT_GENERATED, superheated flag and `runtimeBoost`, and per circuit 0-25 the throttled lEUt, `adjustBurnTimeForConfig` over 1000 loads, water and superheated steam over 4800 ticks; `MTETreeFarm.getTierMultiplier`, the mode multipliers and the tool constants. The steam a tick (`lEUt x 2 x efficiency / 10000` in `onRunningTick`, which needs a tile entity) is read from GT's bytecode, not called. LCE/ECE, XL turbines, reactors stay `not_modelled`. **Fuels per generator** (how many candidates and farms were pruned is printed in the notes): the top 4 by EU per unit of fuel (`candidates`, 1-16); `self_sustaining` first keeps fuels a forward closure over the run's enabled recipes at the tier reaches from the free set and the farms, all of those the fewest steps away, then by EU per fuel. **Fuel chains**: the graph's own and one pinned to each other candidate of the fuel (side routes included: creosote is a pyrolyse oven's byproduct, D189 leaves side routes unexpanded), up to 4; for `self_sustaining` the pins are the candidates whose inputs the closure reaches, nearest first. **Farms** (`gregtec.planner.farms`): DB maps first; GT's Tree Growth Simulator from `gt.recipe.treefarm` (NEI's per-sapling page, mode multiplier applied) x `2t^2-2t+5` (`gtmath.treefarm`, from `MTETreeFarm`) x the tool's multiplier at `VP[t]` for 100 ticks, the sapling (slot 1) a catalyst; tier **LV** (the most logs per EU) and tool an **electric saw** (buzzsaw, x2, its 32 EU per operation charged from the plant): both questions. One farm per product (most per EU), one member per oredict slot. `data/curated/farms.tsv` (rows tagged `source=curated`, cited by file:line) ships **empty**: no crop or mob farm numbers are invented; Clayton or a Lab run fills it. GT is LGPL-3.0: its source was read at tag 5.09.54.265 to learn behaviour; nothing copied | — (scopes WO-4.2) | WO-4.2 |
| D234 | 2026-11-12 | The self-sustaining validator and the bootstrap kit (WO-4.2, plan 7.2 `self_sustaining`, 8 row 4.2) | **Pending reviewer (PR questions).** `gregtec.planner.sustain`. **Free set** default: `fluid:water`, `fluid:air`, `minecraft:cobblestone@0`, `minecraft:stone@0`, `sunlight` (plan 7.2's list; configurable per request and `gos power design --free`; not in gos.toml yet). **In the MILP**, every input outside the free set is banned (farms and slot members added as recipes), so only closed lines are found. **The validator is independent of that ban**: over the solved plan it reads each `r:` node's ports again from the DB with its own SQL (consumed inputs, outputs x chance, by stable key; its EU is the node's sized draw `eut_avg`, GT's math), re-reads each farm from its source, checks each `member:` against `ore_member`, derives a generator's ports again from the DB by the node's `spec`, never from the ports the designer wrote (review MEDIUM-1; `gen_ports`: the cited fuel row's fluid or item and FUEL_VALUE; a single block's fuel row must be in one of the maps that block burns (its `generator.recipe_map` or `machine_map`, as `generators.catalog` builds `fuel_maps`; final check), then GT's `getFuelValue` from `generator_gt`, else WO-4.1's reading of that map's FUEL_VALUE (kept: the designer sizes from the same reading, and a fixture or older export may lack `generator_gt`), at eu_out x amps; a turbine's `generators.turbine` at its rotor and dynamo; boilers' `gtmath.boiler` at their circuit feeding the steam turbine, which must get its flow) and fails when the node declares other ports, has no spec or cites what the DB hasn't; fails on a GT recipe that uses up nothing but gives a non-free item (review MEDIUM-2: an export quirk, never counted on); sums every item at the node's crafts/s and fails on any non-free item short beyond float noise (1e-6 of **that item's own** flow, so a trickle of a dense fuel still counts), on a declared non-free import in `boundary.in`, on an unreadable node, or on net EU/t not above 0. Non-free items left over are listed as `surplus` (and in the kit as `sinks`): in the game they need a void or storage, or the machine stalls. It runs on every option of every mode (`closed` in the plan) and must pass for `self_sustaining`. A rejected candidate is explained by solving it without the ban and listing what the validator finds it would import. **Bootstrap kit**: farm catalysts (saplings, seeds) and tools per farm machine, kept recipe inputs (programmed circuits, moulds: one per machine, GT lists a circuit with amount 0), the generator's parts (rotor, dynamo hatch, throttle circuit), and starter fuel = fuel rate x the sum of every node's cycle time (an upper bound on the time the first fuel takes through the line, labelled so in the kit) | — | WO-4.2 |
| D235 | 2026-11-12 | Objective modes, their ranking and scale bounds (WO-4.2, plan 7.2 table, D11, D13, D62, D67, D198) | **Pending reviewer (PR questions).** Ranking stays in `planner/rank.py` (`order_power`): `best` = weighted score (net EU/t up; effort, upkeep inputs, complexity, scarcity down; each normalised by the largest among the options; `(w_net x net - sum w x cost) / sum w`); `max_output` = net EU/t within the scale bound; `output_per_effort` = net EU/t / effort hours (effort 0, nothing counted, ranks after those with a number, by net EU/t among themselves, with a note); `self_sustaining` = the `best` score among validated lines (the table defines the mode by its constraint; ranking them by the same score is the conservative reading). Ties: fewer new machine types, lower tier, id. Weights: `w_net_eut`, `w_upkeep` new in `me/prefs.md`, `w_effort`/`w_complexity`/`w_scarcity` shared with routes, balanced (1) by default (D67); read from the vault's `me/prefs.md` (read only), else the template's `vault-template/me/prefs.md`, never written. Upkeep = non-free inputs per hour (fluids per 1000 L). **Options**: every pruned (generator, fuel, fuel chain) is solved on its own network with up to 2 chain routes through the same no-good cuts (`solve.routes`), the pool is ranked and the best 2-3 returned, so options differ in the generator, the fuel or a chain primary choice; fewer than 2 found is noted, not padded. **Scale bound**: `unit` (one generator block and its chain, default), `generators` N, `eut_cap` C (the most whole units with net <= C; none if one unit is over), `raw_budget` R per hour (non-free inputs; a closed line isn't bounded by it and gets as many units as the best line the budget bounds, one when none is, so `max_output` never favours a line that imports). The power plan JSON (`kind: power`) reuses the 7.1 plan for the chain (WO-3.4's `_plan`) plus generator/farm/member nodes and a `power` section; persisting it and the ledger are WO-4.4, the view and `power-designer` skill WO-4.6, the effort breakdown UI WO-4.3 | — | WO-4.2 |

Status change and record are one `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction; `base/power.md` is then rewritten through `VaultIO` (atomic, path-guarded, schema-checked; `base-power`'s keys are now defined in `_gos/schema.md`); no vault or a refused note is a reported problem, never a failure (the DB record is the ledger, as D252 treats notes). **Lifecycle (judgement call):** `store.NEXT` now also lets `accepted` and `building` go to `retired` (a line dropped before it runs); every later move is Clayton's, as accepting is (D251): `gos plan --status PLAN building|running|retired`, `POST /api/plans/{id}/status` (admin, paired devices), the Designer's accept with `supersedes` (`gos plan --accept NEW --supersedes OLD`: the old plan retires in the same transaction, so it leaves the ledger; refused whole if the old one can't retire). **No agent writes the ledger or moves a plan:** `ledger_get` is registered (read-only, both lanes); `ledger_update` and `plan_set_status` are not agent tools (catalog `LATER` says why). **Power plans (judgement call):** WO-3.6's `store.save` writes production-shaped exports and notes, so a power plan is persisted for its lifecycle by `store.save_power` (canonical JSON and its `plan` row, `kind = power`, only); `gos power design MODE ... --accept N` accepts option N (`designer.accept_power`): row, `base/lines/ln-pw-<hex>.md` (eut = net, tier = distribution tier) and its source in the ledger; its exports, `plans/` note and a dashboard accept wait for WO-4.6's Power view. **Line outputs (D211, D252):** `MyBase.line_outputs` = each **running** production line's target at its rate, summed per key (accepted or building lines don't produce yet; byproducts stay out: conservative, a question); **never a boundary for the request's own target** (review H1: a running line would otherwise block redesigning, scaling or superseding itself; `graph._Builder`); `GET /api/base` lists them with their `plan:` citations and the Base view's panel (now "Running lines") shows each key, its rate and its plans (needs Clayton's board approval, D92). **Other surfaces:** `gos ledger [--json] [--write]` (exit 1 with a warning); | D239 | 2026-11-12 | The EU ledger's warnings and the OC telemetry hook (WO-4.4, plan 7.2 "Warnings: deficit, amp overload, tier mismatch", "Reconciles with telemetry once OC is live", D71, D219) | **Reviewer agreed (WO-4.4 review, 2026-11-12): base-wide deficit, tier mismatch semantics, amp overload only at sourced tiers and a vault write failure reported, not fatal; telemetry compare-only agreed with one change (review M2, below). Pending Clayton (PR questions).** Computed per scope (`all`, `running`) from the plans' numbers only (`ledger.build`, pure): **`deficit`**: the scope's average use exceeds its generation **base-wide** (GT transformers move EU between tiers, so a per-tier shortfall is the next warning, not a deficit); peak headroom is WO-4.5's buffer sizing. **`tier_mismatch`**: a tier its lines draw at has no source at that tier (its EU has to come through transformers, or a source is missing); a source at a tier with no lines is not warned (stepping down is normal). **`amp_overload`**: at a tier that has a source, the lines' amps (each machine's amps x count, what they can draw at once) exceed the sources' amps there; cables are WO-4.5's, so a tier fed only through transformers is not amp-checked until WO-4.5. Each warning names its scope, tier and plans and is listed in the note, the card's notes, `gos ledger` and `ledger_get`. **Telemetry (a hook, judgement call):** `ledger.reconcile` reads WO-7.1's tables: `tel_machine` rows with status `power` heard within WO-7.1's `STALE_S` (imported, a stale entry is never read), and each one's newest `eu_in`/`eu_out` sample (Computronics' average EU/t in and out) **only if that sample is itself fresh** (review M2: `ts >= last_seen - HEARTBEAT_S`, WO-7.1 rewriting an unchanged reading that often, so an older sample was left out since and is never carried forward), summed (the note says chained entries may double-count) with their `tel:` refs and the oldest sample's age; the note (`tel_eu_in`, `tel_eu_out`, `tel`), the card (source `oc`) and `ledger_get` show them beside the running scope's generation and use. It compares, never corrects, and raises **no warning**: which readings stand for which lines on a given base (an LSC every line feeds through, or not) and any drift alert are WO-7.4/7.5's (D71's power deficit notice); tested on fake telemetry only (Clayton's PC step when OC is live). | Builds on D219 | WO-4.4 |
| D268 | 2026-11-12 | Spoiler levels in code: one global setting, raised only by a whole-utterance command, scoped by code (WO-6.4) | **The setting** is `[policy] spoiler_default` (0 nudge, the default; 1 partial; 2 full; a service key, D72), read by the core, the tool registry and the Quests view (`core/gregtec/quests/spoilers.py`). **Round 4 (2026-11-12, decided by the project manager, pending Clayton, reversible, stricter than before so it fails safe)**: four rounds of sentence grammars and veto lists each left phrasings open (a question about the command, a condition or reported text before it, a label and colon), so they are deleted and replaced by three rules. **(R1) Whole-utterance commands only.** The level rises above the global setting only when the ENTIRE request, after normalisation (case, curly apostrophes, spaces, end punctuation) and a closed set of leading and trailing fillers (hey gos, ok gos, gos, please, pls, ok, okay, um, uh, so, now, then, can you, could you; trailing please, pls, now, then, for me, in gtnh, in game; the router's own filler included), is one of a closed set (`spoilers.FULL_COMMANDS`, `PARTIAL_COMMANDS`): **2** on "spoil it", "spoil that", "spoil this", "spoil me", "spoil it fully", "spoil that fully", "full answer", "the full answer", "give me / i want / id like / i'd like the full answer", "full spoilers", and "spoil (the) <quest name or q:id> (quest) (fully)" (a name of at most eight words, not opening with a pronoun); **1** on "partial answer", "a/the partial answer", "give me a/the partial answer", "i want / id like / i'd like a partial answer", "a bit more", "a little more", "give me a bit more", "a hint", "give me a hint", "more of a hint", "more detail(s)". If the request holds anything else (another sentence, a question, a label and colon, a condition, a quotation, a negation) the level does not rise, and the reply ends "Say 'spoil it' on its own to see more." (`spoilers.HOW_TO_ASK`, when it mentions "spoil", "full answer" or "partial answer" and isn't a refusal). The router routes exactly the closed set to the instant `spoil` intent; nothing else reaches it. **(R2) Scope comes from code, never from the model.** A lane's tool calls (fast and deep) always get the global level, whatever the request says; only the asker's instant command grants more, as a per-quest-id grant in the tool layer (`Registry.call(grant=...)`, `Call.spoil_grant`): a `spoil` argument above a quest's grant is shown at the grant, so a model can't raise anything. "spoil <quest>" grants the quest whose name is exactly those words (any case, no formatting codes) or that id; none or several: it asks back, never guesses. A bare command grants the locked quests the previous reply's code-built card showed (its pre-retrieval card or an instant command's card, kept server-side in the asker's conversation; at most 3, else it asks which), never the ids the model cited or looked up; with no such quest it asks which quest. Raising one quest never raises another: `quest_get` of a granted quest names what it unlocks and its prerequisites at their own level (the global level plus that quest's own grant, never the level asked for the tapped quest, also on the dashboard's `/api/tools` call where his tap may go to full), so the adversarial fake that walks unlock chains at `spoil=2` gets nudges; a property test calls every quest tool the dashboard reaches, at every level, for every locked quest in the fixture book, and finds no other locked quest above its own level. db_sql is never opened by a grant. **(R3) The level travels with the reply.** A spoiler command's reply carries `spoil: {level, quests}`; the Quests view uses it (`GET /api/quests?spoil=L&open=q:..`): only those quests at that level, a partial request stays partial, and the view's own buttons and tap work as before. **Consequence**: "what comes after X? spoil it" no longer raises in one breath; he says "spoil it" on its own after the answer. A general question that gets no quest card ("what does the secret alloy quest need" routes to the general intent) leaves nothing for a bare "spoil it": it asks which quest, and "spoil secret alloy" works. **Unchanged from the earlier rounds**: a tool shows the global level without a `spoil` argument; the list tools are no oracle past the frontier (`quest_for_item` lists a locked quest at 0 only for an item he named, without its name or amount; `quest_find` matches a locked quest's name only when the book shows it, he said the words, now as whole words with no prefix match, or the level is 1 or more; its description only at 2); prerequisites, and `quest_for_item`'s rows of a locked quest whose name it doesn't show, are ordered by id, never by a hidden name; his own taps on the dashboard and the shell may go to full; db_sql refuses quest names, descriptions, task logic, repeat times, tasks and rewards below the full level; questline summaries hold only what level 0 shows; ore locations are exempt (D27). What each level shows is D133's (0: lines, state, prerequisites, `start` rows, its name only when the book shows it; 1 adds its name and tasks; 2 its description and rewards), plus `unlocks` rows. **Tests**: every phrasing from the four review rounds stays at the default; the closed set, with every filler, raises exactly its level and quest; a property test wraps each command in generated prefixes, suffixes, labels, conditions, quotations and questions (over 3,000 sentences) and asserts no rise; end to end, the adversarial fake gets nothing on B1-B5. **Pending Clayton (PR questions)**: (1) the deep lane never spoils; (2) the db_sql guard changes D133's rule; (3) R1's one-utterance rule and which extra phrasings he wants in the closed set; (4) "his own words" for the list tools is a whole-word subset check on his request. | D133 (the spoil default "0 in the tool, not read from `[policy] spoiler_default`"; db_sql reading quest text) | WO-6.4 |

Playwright e2e at 2560x1440 and 390x844 (`dashboard/e2e/quests.spec.ts`: neither the nudge payload nor the page holds a spoiler; tap; Spoil it; item link; contrast in all 12 themes; nothing clipped or sideways on the phone); a quest card's rows now wrap on the phone. **Eval** (`gos eval quest-guide`, `core/gregtec/quests/questeval.py`, `tests/evals/quest_guide.tsv`): 20 questions over the four functions and the tier on a self-authored pack (`tests/evals/quest_pack.py`, never pack data), truth as references into the quest DB (`q<n>`, item keys, `lane=instant`); a row also fails on any spoiler field above its allowed level in the answer or its card. `--fake` runs an adversarial fake model that asks for full spoilers every time and repeats all it reads and, since round 4, walks every quest id it has seen at `spoil=2`; a question may be a conversation (turns split by " | ", the spoiler command its own turn, every earlier reply checked for leaks too); the real run uses the stored key on the fast lane's Haiku, its calls in `llm_usage` as `eval:quest-guide`. **L0 never spoils** is tested for every locked quest of the pack (asked by name, by what comes after it, by how to get there, and by every item it asks for or gives) on answers and cards, and on the view's payload; the final review adds rows with a caveat, a condition or a retraction after the command, a test that a model choosing its own item keys and words (`quest_for_item`, `quest_find`) for a plain L0 request learns nothing of a locked quest, and one that a command naming one quest spoils no other; also over the server harness's real recipe DB, and on GTNH 2.9.0's real book at each stage's key quest (`GOS_QUESTS_PACK`, a PC step). | — | WO-6.4 |
| D240 | 2026-11-12 | Where storage and distribution numbers come from; V10 resolved (WO-4.5, plan 7.2 "Storage and distribution", 13.2 V10, D230) | **Reviewed (advisor): cables changed to exported fields, storage in the power pass agreed, licence agreed.** **Cables (V10, resolved: exported fields; changed after review M1).** Every GT cable and wire is an `MTECable` (GT++'s `GTPPMTECable` extends it; 588 in 2.9.0's harness run). Its tooltip is formatted with gtnhlib's `NumberFormatUtil` in the JVM's locale and labelled in the game language, so on a de-DE or fr-FR Windows `Max Voltage: 2.048` would read as 2 V; the power pass therefore writes each cable's own public fields, `mVoltage`, `mAmperage`, `mCableLossPerMeter` (`storage.cables`, table `cable`), cited `mte:<id>`. For a run made before that (the first WO-4.5 runs), `gregtec.planner.distribution` falls back to the en_US tooltip in `machine.tooltip`: any digit grouping (`,` `.` space, no-break and narrow no-break space, `'`) is stripped, the voltage must be one of GT's and match the tier the line names, and a tooltip that fails is left out and counted in the catalog's notes (`N cable tooltips unreadable`), never guessed. The harness integration test checks that the fallback reads the same three numbers as the fields for all 588 cables. No curated cable table is kept: the fallback was not needed. **Storage (added to GregtecLink's power pass, `gt_power.json` `storage`; schema 5).** Nothing in the DB gave battery charge, buffer slots, transformer amps or LSC cell capacity, so the power pass (one part, `parts.power`) now writes: batteries (every `battery*` oredict member that is an IC2 electric item: tier, max charge, transfer limit, rechargeable by a simulated charge, so GT's one-use acid/mercury batteries and the ZPM module are not storage; a wildcard member, as IC2's RE battery, Advanced RE battery, Energy Crystal and Lapotron Crystal are listed, is read and keyed at meta 0, its tier and capacity not depending on the damage: review M3), battery buffers (tier, slots, voltage, own store), transformers (each read from a new `BaseMetaTileEntity` in both modes, `down` and `up`; bartworks' distributor, which keeps the voltage, is exported but never used as a step) and LSC cells (kekztech's `Capacitor` values, block meta = index + 1 as the LSC reads a stack, glass tier, capacity as a decimal string: some exceed 64 bits). Tables `cable`, `battery`, `battery_buffer`, `transformer`, `lsc_cell`, one set per run like the other power tables; new tables only, so no migration file. The checks read the current export run's rows only (`current_run`; review L4): a newer run without storage gives "no storage data in the current export run", never an older run's rows mixed in. `gos dist` opens the DB read-only, which never upgrades it, so a schema-4 DB reads as having no storage (review M2): `gos dist cable` still works from the tooltips, the others exit 2 with what to do. The harness checks `storage_listed` (cables, batteries, buffers, transformer modes, LSC cells). **Licence:** GT5-Unofficial (LGPL-3) was read for behaviour only (the class files in the dev jar, with `javap`: `PowerNodePath`, `MTEBasicBatteryBuffer`, `MTETransformer`, `MTECable`, `GTModHandler.isElectricItem`, the LSC's `Capacitor`; the review also read GTNHLib's `NumberFormatUtil`, LGPL, the same way); nothing is vendored, and every rule in `distribution.py` is re-implemented from that behaviour. | — (resolves V10) | WO-4.5 |
| D241 | 2026-11-12 | Buffer and LSC sizing for a deficit window (WO-4.5, plan 7.2, D239) | **Reviewed (advisor): tier equality and one amp per battery agreed; battery pick, wildcard batteries and the ledger cap changed; the threshold pending Clayton (PR question).** `distribution.size_buffer`: the window is `(peak - generation) x duration` at a tier, in EU (`x 20` ticks per second). **Battery-buffer option:** the tier's buffer with the most slots, each slot holding the tier's **smallest** rechargeable battery whose buffer count stays at or under `max_buffers`, else, when none does, the one with the most charge (GT's buffer takes only an item whose tier **equals** its own, `GTModHandler.isElectricItem(stack, tier)`; changed after review L1: "most charge" picked UV's Ultimate Battery, about 2^63 EU, for a 6e9 EU window; charge stands in for cost, which isn't modelled); one holds `slots x charge + its own store` and gives `slots` amps at its voltage (GT's `maxAmperesOut` is the battery count); the count is the larger of the energy count and the EU/t count. **LSC option:** the biggest cell at or below the tier cap (default the buffered tier; `--tier-cap`, the ledger view uses the ledger's highest tier), `ceil(EU / cell capacity)` cells, with its glass tier; the LSC's 1%-a-day loss (its tooltip) is noted, not counted. **Recommendation (judgement call; the reviewer agreed as a conservative, adjustable default, pending Clayton):** battery buffers while the window fits in at most `MAX_BUFFERS = 4` of them (`--max-buffers`, at least 1), the LSC beyond that when a cell is available, else battery buffers however many, said so. The LSC needs IV-tier parts and a glass tier, so below EV the switch only ever falls back to buffers. `gos dist` refuses a NaN, infinite or negative amount and a `--max-buffers` below 1 (review L3). **From the ledger** (`gos dist ledger --window S`): per tier, its lines' peak over its own sources' generation; when those add up to more than the base's shortfall (whole peak over whole generation: transformers bring the rest), the base's shortfall is shared out between the short tiers in proportion to their own deficits, so the buffers together store no more than the base needs (changed after review L2: each tier was capped separately, so two short tiers could buffer up to twice it), and no buffer is proposed while the base covers its peak; the duration is always Clayton's input (the ledger has no peak duration). | Builds on D239 | WO-4.5 |
| D242 | 2026-11-12 | Cable runs and transformer placement (WO-4.5, plan 7.2, D239) | **Reviewed (advisor): agreed; the follow-up tool and card pending Clayton (PR question).** **Cable run** (`distribution.check_run`, `gos dist cable --tier T --amps A --segment CABLE=BLOCKS ...`): GT's power path as `PowerNodePath` does it: a packet's voltage drops by every cable's loss per metre over its length (`loss x length`, summed), so `voltage out = max(0, V - loss)`, EU/t lost `= min(loss, V) x amps`; a cable rated below the voltage burns (overvoltage); a run carrying more amps than a cable's rating burns it (amp overload); nothing arriving is a problem too. Exit 1 on any problem. Beside the check it lists the cables of the voltage's own tier (rated `V..4V`) that carry the amps, lowest loss first, the thinnest of each kind: **options, not a pick** (judgement call: what a cable costs to make isn't in this check; superconductors come first at loss 0). **Transformer placement** (`place_transformers`, `gos dist transformer --from S --to L --amps A`): the steps between the tiers, each a transformer of the lower tier in mode `down` (S above L, GT's default) or `up`, counted from the lines back: `ceil(amps / unit amps out)` units, then `ceil(amps x V out / V in)` amps upstream (transformers keep the EU); at each step the unit needing the fewest (then fewer amps out, mte id) is used, every candidate's count listed; units that keep the voltage (bartworks' distributor) are never steps; the candidates are GT's transformers, its power transformers, its Wet Transformer (`MTEWetTransformer`, 64 A out, picked whenever it needs the fewest blocks) and GT++'s hi-amp ones. Half mode (screwdriver) isn't modelled. **From the ledger:** each `tier_mismatch` tier gets the chain from the nearest sourced tier above it (else below) for its lines' amps. Lengths aren't in the ledger, so cable runs are only checked when asked (a layout can feed them later). Surface: `gos dist buffer|cable|transformer|ledger` (`--json`), read-only; no agent tool, card or view yet (judgement call: WO-4.5's acceptance is the three scenarios; a `distribution_check` tool and a ledger card section can follow). | Builds on D239 | WO-4.5 |

| D273 | 2026-11-12 | CropsNH's rules in code, and what V30 and V32 come to (WO-6.7, plan 7.17, D78) | `gregtec.crops.rules` re-implements CropsNH 2.0.140's breeding, growth and machine behaviour (source read at tag `2.0.140`, commit `db0a9936bdcc`; **LGPL-3.0: read to learn the behaviour, nothing copied or vendored**) with the 2.9.0 pack's `config/cropsnh/Configuration.cfg` (modpack tag `2.9.0`, commit `79f9a03`: every value is the default). **Breeding** (`TileEntityCropSticks.attemptBreeding`): an empty double stick tries 1 growth tick in 3 (every 256 ticks); half the time it copies a neighbour that can cross (each equally likely), otherwise (or when none can) the neighbours at their breeding threshold match parent-combination recipes (`MutationRegistry`: the distinct species sorted by registration order, a recipe matching when its parents are the first two to four of them; one match chosen at random; a Crop Breeder-only match falls through) and then mutation pools (a random pool with two or more of the neighbours, duplicates counted, then a random member). The child must grow on the double stick (soil, sub-soil, requirements). **Stats** (`variateStat`): the contributing parents' average (integer division) plus one draw from the config's lists (breeding: -1 -1 0 1 1 below 50 fertilizer, -2..2 from 50, 0 0 0 1 2 3 4 from 101; spreading: -1 0 0, 0, and the high list), clamped 1..31: repeats weigh more, which plan 7.17's summary leaves out. **Growth** (`getNutrientsPerCycle`, `getGrowthRate`) with Java's int and float arithmetic. **Weeds**: spread when Resistance < Growth and Growth > the crop's `weeds_above` (24; Necrobloom 8), at `rand(50) - Growth <= 2` a tick; an empty stick grows one 1 in 100. **Seed back**: removing a crop returns its seed when Resistance > rand(31) (1/31 at Resistance 1), which the keep rule says. **Machines** (`MTECropBreeder`, `MTESeedGenerator`, `MTECropManager`; tanks from GT's `getCapacityForTier`): Crop Breeder 40% at LV +10% a tier to 100%, a failure eats the inputs, 144 mB x child tier + 72 mB x stat points, a fifth of a basic machine's tank below IV (so LV holds 3,200 mB: that tank is the LV/MV "cannot breed high-stat seeds" cap), 3 slots below HV, the child at the recipe parents' average; Seed Generator 36 mB a stat point, a tenth of the tank below IV; Crop Manager radius 3 + 2 x tier, 2 up and down, fertilizer to 200 (its water, fertilizer and Weed-EX switches start off: `MTECropManager`). **Upkeep** (`onGrowthTick`): every growth tick a stick uses 1 water and 1 fertilizer while it has any, crop or not; rain adds water. **Checked against the mod**: unit vectors worked by hand from that behaviour (tests/unit/crops/test_rules.py); `getNutrientsPerCycle`, `getGrowthRate` and `variateStat` also run from CropsNH's own dev jar by an oracle, as D174's for GT (`tests/unit/crops/oracle/CropsNHOracle.java` writes `tests/unit/crops/vectors/cropsnh.tsv`, 3,310 rows over liked tags, humidity, sky, water, fertilizer, tier, Growth and parent stats; `test_rules_oracle.py` replays every row, all equal; added after review); and on the client harness export every Crop Breeder's chance and tank, every Seed Generator's tank and Crop Manager's range in CropsNH's own tooltips, and the fertilizer NEI shows on all 109 Crop Breeder recipes (made at 31/31/31) equal the rules (tests/integration/test_crop_guide_harness.py). **V30** and **V32**: partly resolved from source and the export (plan 13.2); the Lab checks are in `docs/runbooks/crop_guide.md`. **Judgement call**: recipe matching depends on registration order, which the export doesn't carry; a layout holds exactly one recipe's parents, and when a smaller recipe of them might also match, the chance shown is its lowest and the step says so. | Refines plan 7.17 (stat steps), V30, V32 | WO-6.7 |
| D274 | 2026-11-12 | The breeding guide: path, steps, citations, what he has (WO-6.7) | **Pending reviewer (PR questions).** `gregtec.crops.planner.guide(target)` starts from the crops Clayton lists (`kv` `crops.have`, each with its best Growth/Gain/Resistance) plus any crop an alternate seed plants (an obtain step), and picks, by code, the path with the fewest mutation-pool steps, then the fewest Crop Breeder steps above his tier, then the fewest steps, then the shortest expected time (plan 7.17: recipes first, then pools; a model explains, never reorders). Steps: obtain, crop sticks (a 3x3 layout: parents north, south, east, west; what goes under each stick), mutation pool (**judgement call**: two parents, possibly two seeds of one crop, picked among a pool's four cheapest members for the best chance, never a pair a recipe would catch), Crop Breeder (Crop Breeder-only recipes; a crop-stick step notes the Breeder when his tier runs its recipe), and from LV a Crop Manager step (done once My base lists one). Each has requirements (`met` / `needed` / `check`: soil, sub-soil block, met when My base stocks one of its blocks, light, nutrients against the tier's need, catalysts, analyzed seeds), odds and times (chance per growth tick, parents' time to their breeding threshold, expected and 90% times, other children to clear), warnings (weeds, extra neighbours, weedy parents, failed Breeder runs) and the keep rule; then a stats plan to 31/31/31 (expected children per stat with two parents and the keep rule, as a Markov chain, and the time). **Assumed setup** (judgement call, shown in the view; corrected after review): the times are the **best case** for water and fertilizer, kept topped up (100 fertilizer by hand; 200 only once My base lists a Crop Manager, whose step says to switch its water, fertilizer and Weed-EX on in its GUI and fill them), and the worst case for the biome (dry, none liked), with sky access. A stick uses 1 water and 1 fertilizer every growth tick (about 21 min from 100), so without a Crop Manager each nutrients requirement also shows the dry score and is `check` when the crop would slow or sicken once they run out, and the stats plan and keep rule draw from the low list (fertilizer gone), with a line for the -2..+2 list kept by topping up to 100 at least every 51 growth ticks (about 11 min). A parent that doesn't grow at the setup gets no time, and the guide no total. A mutation pool path at most half the guide's total time is noted, never chosen (the reviewer's suggestion). **Citations**: every number is a `Num` carrying the `crop:` ids it was computed from, an `r:` for a Crop Breeder recipe's time, EU/t and catalysts, and **`mte:` for a CropsNH machine's numbers** (judgement call: plan 8 names crop:, r: and plan:; a machine's chance, tank and range are facts of the machine, and mte: is D131's id for them); `planner.check` fails an uncited number, a line whose own digits aren't in the DB rows it cites (a crop's name, requirement texts, sub-soil blocks and exported numbers; a Breeder recipe; a machine), and any id the DB lacks. CropsNH's rule constants (a growth tick every 256 ticks, a weed 1 in 100, the 90% figure) are no DB row: their numbers cite the crop or machine they apply to, and `gregtec.crops.rules` names where each comes from. **His crops and target** are Clayton's edits (the Crops view, `gos crops have|target`, which refuse a name that matches several crops), never an agent's (as D212). Tools `crop_find`, `crop_plan`, `crop_step` (both lanes; `crop_status` stays WO-7.6's); the `crop-breeder` skill joins the fast lane's cached prompt; "next crop step" is an instant command (no model, works with AI off) answering the next step's title and first number with its refs; `gos crops guide|have|target|check`. | Refines plan 7.0 (crop tools), 7.17 | WO-6.7 |

| D257 | 2026-11-12 | The game watchers and their events (WO-6.1, plan 5.4, 5.5, 7.5, V05, V06, V27) | **Pending reviewer (PR questions).** `gregtec.watchers`: one process (`python -m gregtec.watchers`, `gos watch run --instance play\|lab`) of small readers, each `poll() -> events`, never holding a handle. **Reads:** every game file through `gamestate.safe_open` only (logs: stat, open, seek, read, close; saves: whole-file `read_bytes`, 3 tries, a parse error retried), and a save file is read only once its size and mtime have held still for 1 s (`Settle`), so a read never lands inside the game's save burst and the next save is ~45 s away. **Logs** (poll 1 s): `latest.log` and `fml-client-latest.log` both, same rules, a line the other log already had counted once (keyed by stamp, kind, data and the file, so the same text twice in one second in one log is kept twice; review L4) (so V05's routing doesn't matter); a tail starts at the log's end (the last session's lines are never new) unless its saved offset (`GOS_HOME/data/watchers/tails.json`) is for the same file; rotation = a new file identity or a smaller size. Lines give `chat`, `death` (the tracked player's name, from the login line, then one of vanilla 1.7.10's death wordings; a player's own `<name>` chat never counts), `vein_found` (VisualProspecting's en_US notices), `location` (the login line), `crash` (crash-report lines), `error` (ERROR/FATAL in latest.log, FATAL only in the FML log, which mods flood; once a minute per message, at most 30 an hour). **Saves** (poll 2 s): level.dat `Data.Player` (else the only `playerdata/<uuid>.dat`) gives `dim_change`, `location` (moved 8+ blocks, at most every 30 s) and `item_delta` (the inventory diff); BQ progress gives `quest_done` `q:<id>` (the existing ingest); `stats/<uuid>.json` gives `item_delta` per action (crafted, mined, used, broken; registry name with meta null, since stats ignore meta) and `death`; ServerUtilities' `MC/logs/world.log` gives `block_placed`/`block_broken` (display name and meta as text, never a key; `block_broken` added beside plan 7.5's `block_placed`); the VP **client** cache gives `vein_found` (the server cache is every vein, D27, so it never means "found"); JourneyMap Death waypoints give `death` with where (Nether x and z /8); `crash-reports/` gives `crash`; GOS's own stale flag gives `export_stale`. The first read of each is its baseline. A death seen by several readers within 120 s keeps one id: the later ones carry `same_as`. **Item keys** (CLAUDE.md, D104): registry name + meta + NBT hash in NEI's `NBTJson` typing (`watchers/nbtjson.py`: typed values over `ingest/nbt.py`'s parser, Java's float text), numeric ids (EndlessIDs' `idExt` first) named only through the world's own `FML.ItemData`; an id it doesn't name is counted `unnamed` and dropped. **Text** from the game is cleaned (colour codes, control and direction characters, 500 characters) and marked `untrusted`: data, never instructions. **Delivery:** every event is appended to `GOS_HOME/data/events/<date>.jsonl` (kept for WO-6.3's store) and posted by a sender thread to `POST /api/game/events` with the `watchers` client token from Clayton's keyring (`gos token new watchers`; without one, spool only), in batches of at most 100 events and 128 KB, with the paused state and a 10 s heartbeat; a failed post keeps the events (2000, oldest dropped) and backs off. The core keeps the last 500 in memory (`GET /api/game/events`) until WO-6.3. gos-dev's `gos watch run --instance play` refuses (CLAUDE.md). **Licences:** nothing vendored; formats from research B ([B§1-6]) and the existing MIT-licensed BQ and VP readers | Refines plan 5.4 and 7.5 (`block_broken`; both client logs; stats as `item_delta`) | WO-6.1 PR |
| D258 | 2026-11-12 | The paused state (V29, plan 5.6 AFK, D46) | **Pending reviewer (PR questions).** Resolved from what the game writes, not from GregtecLink: 1.7.10's `IntegratedServer.tick` logs `Saving and pausing game...` on the Server thread on the first tick that sees the game paused (the Esc menu, single-player, not open to LAN), saves (its `Saving chunks for level ...` lines, level.dat, BQ, stats) and stops ticking; **nothing is written when it unpauses** [B§2.2, B§6.1]. So: **paused** from the pause line; **not paused** at the first latest.log Server-thread line that only a ticking server writes (an allow-list, `logparse.TICKING`: a player's chat `<name> text` or `* name action`, an admin command's feedback `[name: text]`, joined/left the game, lost connection, a login) stamped at least 3 s after the pause line by the log's own `[HH:mm:ss]` (the read time only without a stamp) (review H1: the pause save is one long tick on a GTNH world, so vanilla's `Can't keep up! ... Running Nms behind` and mods' warnings come after the pause line while the game is still paused; they never count, and neither does any line of the FML log), or when level.dat is written again more than 10 s after the pause line (the next autosave, at most 45 s after unpausing: GTNH ships Hodgepodge's `autoSaveInterval` = 900 ticks); **unknown** (None) when the world closes (`Stopping server`). A pause line while already paused (a missed unpause) restarts `since`. The core shows `game.paused` only while the watchers are heard (30 s) and their instance's client runs, else None, so a stopped watcher never leaves "paused" on the dashboard; a change pushes the state at once. The path to the dashboard is one log poll (1 s) + one POST + an immediate push (tested with a fake clock, and with the real loop in under 5 s). Not chosen: a GregtecLink client hook (exact both ways, but a mod change and a jar in Play for what the log already says) and OC telemetry going quiet (OC also stops on a lag spike). **Lab test** (runbook `watchers.md`): the log line's delay, that the chip stays on through 60 s of pause, and that the unpause shows within 45 s; if the line comes late (log4j buffering), a GregtecLink client signal is the fallback | Resolves V29 in code (Lab check pending) | WO-6.1 PR |
| D259 | 2026-11-12 | Watchers and voice start and stop with Play (D77, D249, plan 4.2) | **Pending reviewer (PR questions).** The supervisor manages two with-Play processes: `watchers` (`python -m gregtec.watchers --supervised`) and, with `[startup] voice_with_play` (Appendix C's key, now read; default true), `voice` (`python -m gregtec_voice run --supervised`, the WO-5.1 client). They don't start at login or on `gos serve`. Play's pre-launch hook (and Lab's, for the Lab test) calls the supervisor's new `POST /play {"event": "launch", "instance"}` (admin token from `run/supervisor.json`; the hook runs as Clayton) after its lock and Export checks pass, which starts them with `GOS_PLAY_INSTANCE`; post-exit calls `{"event": "exit"}` once the export question is answered or has timed out (plan 4.2), which stops them (stdin `stop`, then a kill after 5 s). The notice is best effort (3 s timeout): GOS not running or not answering is a line in the hook's output, and the game launches anyway (plan 0.1 principle 9). **No post-exit** (Prism runs it only after a clean exit, D121): every 15 s while they run the supervisor lists GTNH clients; once the instance's client has gone (after it was seen, or 5 minutes after the launch, since Prism logs in and updates after pre-launch) for 6 minutes (the question's 5 and one more), it stops them itself. Exit code 2 (no token, no game folder, not Windows, gos-dev on Play) leaves a with-Play process stopped, its reason in the status table, instead of a restart loop. Launching another instance while one plays restarts them for it. **Play gate** (review M1): `[startup] watchers_with_play` (new key, `"lab"` or `"play"`, default `"lab"`): Play's hooks are installed already (WO-1.4), so without it the first deploy would watch Play's next launch before the Lab checks; with `"lab"` the supervisor answers Play's launch with `ignored` and the reason (the hook prints it) and starts nothing; Clayton sets `"play"` once runbook §2–§4 pass on Lab. `POST /restart` of a with-Play process while nothing plays is a 409, and the watchers run under the supervisor with no `GOS_PLAY_INSTANCE` exit 2 instead of defaulting to Play (review L1). On stop the sender gets 1 s and its last post 1.5 s, inside the supervisor's 5 s (review L2). **Known gap** (review L7): a supervisor that restarts mid-session (a crash, `gos serve` again) doesn't know the instance is playing, so the watchers and voice stay stopped until its next launch; adopting a running client at start is left out, since it would start watching an instance without its hook | Implements D77's "voice and the game watchers start with Play"; carries out D249's move | WO-6.1 PR |
| D260 | 2026-11-12 | Machine events: GregtecLink's listener, its events file and the tile-entity census (WO-6.2, plan 5.4, D70, V26, [QA:D6, S7]) | **Pending reviewer (PR questions).** **Listener** (`mod/`, `listener` module; refused when `export` is on, so never in Export): Forge `BlockEvent.PlaceEvent`/`BreakEvent` at `LOWEST`, cancelled events not received, for GT's machine block only; each is noted and settled at the end of the server tick against the world (placement: a `BaseMetaTileEntity` with `mID > 0` stands there; break: the machine noted at the break is gone), so a placement or break another mod cancels is never reported. Read only: a loaded chunk's tile-entity map is read directly (never `World.getTileEntity`, which creates a missing tile entity), no chunk is loaded, nothing is added (no items, blocks, mixins, commands or packets). Cables and pipes (`BaseMetaPipeEntity`) are not machines, here or in the census (*judgement call*: plan 5.4 names `BaseMetaTileEntity`; counting every cable would swamp My base). **V26 at the pin** (read from GT 5.09.54.265's classes): the machine's id is `BaseMetaTileEntity.mID` (written as an int `mID` tag), the block is `gregtech:gt.blockmachines` for every GT, GT++, bartworks and TecTech machine, and its item meta is the mID, so an event names the machine as registry name + meta (`gregtech:gt.blockmachines@1000`) and `mte:<id>`, never a numeric block or item id; v1.0's EndlessIDs block decoder is not added. **Delivery** (*judgement call*: the plan leaves it open): one JSON line per event in `<.minecraft>/gregteclink/events/machines.jsonl` (contract `docs/contracts/gregteclink-events-v1.md`: v, kind, boot, seq, ts_ms, dim, x, y, z, mte_id, registry, meta; open-append-close through `PathGuard`; rotated to `machines.1.jsonl` over 1 MiB at start), which WO-6.1's watchers tail like a log (`safe_open`, saved offsets, from its start the first time) and post to `POST /api/game/events` as `machine_built`/`machine_removed` (source `listener`, id `m<boot>-<seq>`, so a re-read line is one event). Not chosen: an HTTP post from the game (a token and a network client inside Play for what the watchers' existing path already carries). Lines are checked field by field (exact field set, `meta == mte_id`, ranges, no `id` key); anything else is dropped as a problem, and line text is data only. **Census** (`gregtec.watchers.census`, in the watchers process): Anvil region files of the overworld and every `DIM<n>`, re-implemented from the format; a region is looked at once its stamp has held still 1 s, then only its 8 KB header (`files.read_range` through `safe_open`) and only the chunks whose location or save time changed; a chunk is decompressed and checked (length within its sectors, gzip/zlib whole, and for chunks with machines a whole NBT compound whose `Level.xPos/zPos` are its own), and a chunk without the bytes `BaseMetaTileEntity` is never parsed; a bad read is retried on the next poll and after 3 tries left until it changes, as a problem. It sends `machine_census` events per region (`scope: chunks`: the chunk indices looked at and every machine in them as `[x, y, z, mte_id, active, shutdown]`; each event is kept under 48 KB of JSON, measured as it is built (the core's feed takes census events up to 64 KB and keeps only their counts), and a chunk with more machines than one event holds goes as one event and then `append: true` events for the same chunk, whose rows the core adds without clearing the chunk again (review M1)), the first look at a world covering every chunk, empty ones included, then one `scope: world` event listing the regions that exist; a region file deleted later sends its known chunks once more with no machines, so the core drops them (review L4). Known chunk slots are not saved across watcher starts, so each start reads the whole world again (the safety net for anything lost while the watchers were down). Each poll reads for at most 0.5 s, so a big world's baseline spreads over polls without holding up the log readers. `gos watch census --instance lab\|play [--world] [--json]` prints a whole census (counts by `mte:<id>` with names from the DB; gos-dev refused on Play). **Licences:** GT, Forge and MC are linked only; the region and NBT formats are re-implemented; nothing vendored | Implements plan 5.4's listener and census; resolves V26 in the harness (Lab check pending) | WO-6.2 PR |
| D261 | 2026-11-12 | My base's automatic machine counts: positions, precedence, Play only (WO-6.2, D211, WO-3.8) | **Pending reviewer (PR questions).** The core keeps each sighting by position in a new core table `my_base_machine_at(source event\|census, dim, x, y, z, mte_id, upd)` (`gregtec.base.seen`; `store.py`'s DDL) and derives the `event` and `census` rows of WO-3.8's `my_base_machine` from it, so D211's table and precedence (manual > census > event) are unchanged. `machine_built` puts the machine at its position, `machine_removed` takes it off only if it is the machine there (a removal of something never seen placed is ignored), so a replayed events file or a retried post changes nothing; `machine_census` replaces the census of the chunks it names, and the baseline's world event drops the census of regions no longer there. Recounted machines: an `event` count of 0 is deleted (lowest precedence anyway); a `census` count of 0 is kept (*judgement call*), so the census, the truth of the last save, still outranks an event count the listener left stale (a machine blown up); the cost is that a machine placed after the last save shows the census's older count until the next save (at most ~45 s with Hodgepodge's autosave). **Only Play's** watchers' posts update My base (`instance == "play"`, *judgement call*): Lab is a test copy, so its events and census are kept in the event feed and shown by `gos watch census --instance lab`, never counted as Clayton's base. A batch is one `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction on the core's own connection, run off the event loop. Every machine event of a post is applied, not only the ones the feed hadn't seen (each is keyed by position, so applying it again in order changes nothing); a failed write answers 503 and the watchers keep the batch and send it again (review M2). While the watchers' sender is behind (1,000 queued, half its queue), the events file and the census are left unread (offset and known chunks kept), so a core that is down can't make the full queue drop them; the log readers go on. The designer reads the result through `MyBase.from_db` as before (owned machines: the count in use, > 0) | Extends D211 (no schema change to `my_base_machine`); adds `my_base_machine_at` | WO-6.2 PR |
| D262 | 2026-11-12 | The listener harness and the Lab check (WO-6.2 acceptance, V26) | **Pending reviewer (PR questions).** `./gradlew runServer -Pharness -PharnessListener` (listener on, export off; keeps the export harness's runs) has a Forge fake player place 10 GT machine kinds on the superflat world through the item's real path (`tryPlaceItemIntoWorld`, Forge's `PlaceEvent`): the EBF (mte 1000, a multiblock controller), hatch 66 and the first 8 single-block machine classes in GT's registry; break 4 (controller, hatch, two machines) through `tryHarvestBlock` (`BreakEvent`); one placement and one break cancelled by another handler at `HIGHEST`; one machine placed and one removed with no player event. After each phase it saves (`saveAllChunks`, then waits for the chunk writer) and copies the region files and the base to `gregteclink/listener-harness/`. Java checks the events file (exactly 10 built then 4 removed, right ids, `registry` + `meta`, one boot, seq 1..14, no numeric id key, nothing from the cancelled ones), that reading never loads a chunk and that nothing is written outside `gregteclink/`; `tests/integration/test_machine_events_harness.py` checks the census of each phase against the base (11, then 6 machines, the unseen ones included) and runs both through the watchers into My base on a DB built from the export harness's run. The cancelled break is posted as `ForgeHooks.onBlockBreakEvent` posts it, since its cancelled path sends the player a packet a fake player has no connection for, so the cancelled `tryHarvestBlock` path itself is checked only on Lab. The second save waits at least 1.1 s of wall time after the first (Anvil save times are whole seconds; the census sees a chunk as changed by its slot), and a check confirms that every chunk phase B changed has a later save time in `census-b` (review H1). CI runs it in the `mod` job after the export harness (about 1.5 min). **Lab check** (runbook `machine_events.md`, PC step): Clayton copies the deployed jar into Lab with `listener=true` and `[sync.lab] exclude` for `mods/gregteclink*` and `config/gregteclink*` (*judgement call*: no install script yet; Lab is only Clayton's), places and breaks 10 machines in `lab-structures`, reads `machines.jsonl` and `gos watch census --instance lab`. **Play** gets the listener later through a script Clayton runs (not in this WO; the census already counts Play's machines from its saves once the watchers run on Play) | Acceptance evidence for WO-6.2; V26 in the harness | WO-6.2 PR |
